# Changelog

All notable changes to the agent-webhook-bridge are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

See [`../VERSIONING.md`](../VERSIONING.md) for the changelog policy — it owns it, including how `[Unreleased]` entries are folded into a version heading at release time.

> This repository's git history begins at **v0.12.0**. The bridge existed earlier (v0.1–v0.11, a Python-consumer + PHP-receiver implementation), but that history was not carried into this repository. The design rationale that is still load-bearing for the current code is preserved in [`../CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md`](../CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md); v0.12.0 itself is recorded in **DL-001**.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **card#8470 (DL-341)** — **a coordination card's name is now restamped when its GitHub issue is retitled — but only a name the bridge still owns.** The `coord-card-create` sibling of DL-328's dependabot retitle arm, which DL-328's own alternatives bullet deferred and named. ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK WRITES** (operator-approved): the first card-`name` write this handler makes after create. No migration, no new or changed config key, no `.env` change, no webhook-subscription change, no new board custom field, and nothing the receiver accepts or rejects moves.
  - **The defect:** a coord card was named once at birth and never again, so an issue retitled in place left its card asserting a title the thread had moved on from — for the life of the card, and to every reader that keys on the card title.
  - ⭐ **The ownership test is BYTE-EQUALITY with what the bridge itself stamped**, adopted from DL-328 rather than re-derived: GitHub's `changes.title.from` on the `issues.edited` event IS the string the bridge wrote at mint time, so a card whose name still equals it byte for byte has been touched by nobody since, and a card whose name differs by so much as a space is LEFT ALONE and the non-write is recorded. ⛔ **The naive "always write the upstream title" fix is refused by name**: the classifier delivers that title on every subsequent event, so an unconditional write would destroy a deliberate human rename on the next webhook. A paired control test asserts the human-renamed card is untouched.
  - **The correlation key is derived from `changes.title.from`, not from the new title** — `id:<sid>` reads the anchored `[PREFIX]`, so a `[QUERY]`→`[TASK]` edit moves the sid and keying on the new title would search for a tag no card carries.
  - ⚑ **It writes `{name}` and MOVES NOTHING, and that is the ruling the card asked for.** `CoordLaneStages::governs()` gates the coord lane model on the anchored `[TASK]` title, so a retitle across that prefix changes which model would govern the thread — and the restamp still does not re-derive a stage: a coord-card move is a separate opt-in (`move_coord_cards`, DL-200) this arm does not run under, a title edit declares no lane (a derivation would demote an unlabelled card to the `later` default on a rename), and the un-governing direction's only target is the fixed create stage, which is a rewrite of wherever the card sits. Pinned by a test on a lane-model board.
  - ⚠ **Bounds, stated:** the evidence lives on the rename event, so a retitle the bridge never received is not repaired here; the **by-ref population** (`issue_population: all`, a non-prefixed issue) carries no `id:` tag and is not covered; a prefix-changing retitle leaves the `id:<sid>` TAG stale (a pre-existing divergence, not one this leg introduces); and **a PINNED card is restamped** — this handler consults no pin on either arm, exactly as DL-328's restamp does not, and whether a hold should stop a name write is a ruling nobody has made.
  - **The `changes.title.from` reader is HOISTED at its second caller** into `App\Bridge\Support\TitleChangeEvidence` — DL-328's copy now delegates to it, with no behaviour change. Two copies of the rule a card-name write is gated on is two places for it to drift, and a drift there does not fail loudly: it writes a name onto a card the bridge may not own. The ACTION gate stays with each classifier, which is where each family's declared event set comes from.
  - ⚠ **Token scope:** `{name}` is a `task.update` write (kanban `TaskPolicy::update` → `BoardPermissions::TASK_UPDATE`), distinct from `task.move`, and it is the FIRST such write on the coord-card arm — whose other writes are a create and the collapse's archive. A writeback token on a custom board role without `update` 403s on every restamp, permanently, surfaced as `coord_card_create_4xx`; a Member-role token is unaffected. Declared on `docs/kanban-integration-contract.md` §2's PATCH row beside DL-328's.

- **card#8423** — **`.release-pr.json` now declares `card_token_regex` and `promote.source`, so release tooling can correlate this repo's `card#`-titled PRs and can only ever promote THIS repo's cards.** **No app code, no operator-facing config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change, and nothing the receiver accepts or rejects moves** — both keys are read only by the toolkit's release tools.
  - **`card_token_regex: "card#[0-9]+"` closes a SILENT gap.** `card#<id>` is now the dominant PR-title correlation form here and `DL-NNN` the exception, but the config declared only `ref_token_regex`, so `release-pr-body` derived card ids from nothing. **Measured on this repo's own last release:** over `v0.79.0..v0.80.0` the shipped-cards manifest was **absent entirely** (0 `<!-- release-manifest:shipped-cards -->` lines) and is now emitted carrying **8336, 8375, 8351, 8286, 8306, 8304, 8305, 8294** — every `card#`-titled PR in the range, none of which carries a `DL-` token. There was no error to notice: the `## Card coverage` section read *All shipped refs have a tracking card* in both runs, because the refs it could see were the two `DL-` ones.
  - ⛔ **It does NOT change what `promote-released-cards` MOVES.** That tool never reads this key and deliberately refuses to derive card ids from commit subjects — a descriptive citation (*"supersedes card#1234"*) is not a claim that the commit ships it, and an id IS the match, so a passing mention would relocate an unrelated card. `card_token_regex` is read by `release-pr-body` only; promotion correlates on the `(#NNN)` squash marker against `payload.pr_number`. What the key feeds is the machine-readable `shipped-cards` manifest, which went from 0 to 1 line carrying the eight ids above.
  - **`promote.source: "PupFuzz/agent-webhook-bridge"` is REQUIRED by the toolkit as of its card#8421, and it is landed here BEFORE the pin that needs it.** `release-promote-cards.yml` pins the promote action at `v0.31.0`, which predates that change, so nothing is red today; against the current toolkit the pre-edit config **refuses at rc 2 before any board read** (measured) — every future release would have promoted nothing, loudly. Bumping the pin without this key is what that failure would have looked like.
  - ⭐ **Why the repo slug and not `"*"` — `"*"` would have been a FALSE declaration.** `"*"` DECLARES that the board tracks exactly one repo; board 8 carries at least one card whose by-ref source is `pupfuzz/agent-roundtable`, so it does not (census on card#8423). Naming the repo qualifies every `dl_number`/`pr_number` match against the card's own by-ref source — those are **repo-scoped counters**, so the guard is what stops a colliding number from moving another repo's card; on this board today the foreign card carries no ref number at all, so the justification is the false declaration, not a reachable collision. **It costs nothing forward:** all **7** cards currently in Shipped-to-Dev (stage 52) carry a correct by-ref source, and a `v0.79.0..v0.80.0` dry-run promotes identically under both values.
  - ⚠ **One historical consequence, in the loud direction:** a ref whose only match is a card with **no derivable by-ref source** is now **named and skipped** instead of moved. Over the full history that is 97 already-released refs; live, it is `DL-23` (card #2132). The remedy is on the card, not the guard — stamp it with `kbcard patch --pr-url`.
- **card#8351 (DL-324)** — **a CI check now fails a PR whose markdown shell fences resolve a secret VALUE onto a readable surface — shipped as a labelled BACKSTOP, never as the rule.**
  - ⚠ **THIS CHANGES WHAT CI REJECTS** (operator-approved): a new always-running workflow, **`doc-fence secret backstop`**, runs `bin/check-doc-secret-fences.py` over every `*.md` and exits non-zero on a secret value reaching stdout, an argv, a log, or shell history from inside a shell code fence. No app code, no config key, no migration, no receiver accept/reject change.
  - ⛔ **A GREEN RUN OF IT IS NOT EVIDENCE THE CLASS IS ABSENT, and the tool prints that sentence on every clean run.** The rule is `docs/config-schema.md` § *Handling a secret VALUE*, stated over the **surface** because the next instrument that resolves a secret is never on anyone's list; this program greps a finite list of spellings underneath it — **with one leg deliberately excepted:** where a pipeline carrying a secret VALUE *ends* is decided **deny-by-default**, because the enumeration of *leaking* tails it replaced had silent gaps and one added pipe stage turned a caught leak into a clean run (`base64 <secret file>` red, `cat <secret file> | base64` green — likewise `| sed`, `| awk`, `| cut`, `| grep`, `| jq`). A tail now leaks unless it is a known stdin **sink**, so that leg is wrong in the loud, waivable direction; re-measured over 85 tags / 1,973 tag-file pairs it reds on nothing the enumerating version did not. **Membership of that sink list has a stated admission test** — an instrument the rule prescribes, or one this repo's runbooks use — because a sink admitted on plausibility is a SILENT green: `dd` with no `of=`, `gpg -d` and `ssh host cat` were all listed as sinks while copying stdin straight to stdout.
  - ⚠ **Four more shapes red that did not** (same operator approval, same loud direction): a pipeline wrapped across a **blank or comment line** after a trailing `|`; a tail reached through **`xargs`**, which converts stdin into the argv `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` publishes; a tail that **compares against a secret file** (`printf … | diff - <secret file>` — the *"Compare both values"* class DL-321 fixed, arriving through a pipe); and a **waiver carried across a blank line**, which now claims only the line immediately below it. A fifth followed from the same round: the splitter treated every `{`/`}` as the shell's group-command syntax, so an unquoted `echo ${SECRET}` was cut into `echo $` + `SECRET` and went green while its quoted twin reddened — braces now separate only as standalone words.
  - ⚠ **A third round added three more, same operator approval and the same loud direction — two of them a question with two different answers, and one a rule that could not fire.** *Does this command put a secret FILE on stdout* had **two divergent copies** of its predicate: the pipeline reader asked over the command’s arguments **and its input redirects**, the command-substitution reader over the arguments alone. So `cat < <secret file>` reddened on its own line and went **green the moment it was captured** — `curl -H "… $(cat < <secret file>)"` was clean while the `$(cat <secret file>)` spelling of the same act reddened, a redirection character deciding a security verdict. **One predicate now answers for both callers**, and it covers bash’s `$(<file)` as well, whose parsed command has an empty name and the path only in the redirect — a spelling the tool had never handled, so the BOUNDS docstring’s claim that the reader understands *command substitution, redirection* was false for exactly these shapes and is now made true rather than narrowed — and **here-strings were the third false conjunct of that same sentence**, closed by the fourth round below. Separately, **`printenv <secret>` and `declare -p <secret>` could not fire at all**: both are printing commands, but the `stdout` arm asked for an argument carrying a `$`-expansion and these take a bare NAME — so they were green while the tool’s own vocabulary already called that name a secret. The bare name is now tested on the `stdout` leg **only**, because what `printenv` puts in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is the name and an `argv` finding there would print a false sentence. Re-measured, the census is **unchanged and byte-identical** (85 tags / 1,973 tag-file pairs / 399 findings / 5 distinct messages), so nothing historical reds and nothing caught was lost. **Four more bounds are DISCLOSED rather than closed**, each a further change to what CI rejects and none of them firing on any doc here: a fence inside a **blockquote** is not recognised as a fence at all; a **Pandoc/Quarto `{bash}` info string** is not a shell fence; there is **no fd table**, so `exec 3<` a secret file followed by `cat <&3` is green; and a **`sh -c` / `bash -c` string operand** is not recursed into, exactly as `eval`’s is not.
  - ⚠ **A fourth round found the two worst silent greens of the set, same operator approval and the same loud direction — an APOSTROPHE and a HERE-STRING.** *(1)* One quote-state walk existed in **eight hand-rolled copies**, and the two that decide a verdict disagreed with the six that do not: `_secret_expansions` — which every value-bearing rule is built on — and `_mask_substitutions` toggled on any `'`, including one inside `"…"` where bash holds it literal, so an apostrophe opened a single-quoted region the shell does not have and **everything after it went green**. `echo "Save this token securely: $BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOKEN"` — DL-321's own live member — reddened, and `echo "Don’t lose it — save this token: $BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOKEN"` did not; the same silent green covered `argv`, `log`, `probe` and the pipeline tail, and through the masker it reached ACROSS commands on one logical line. The copies are **deleted**, not corrected: one `QuoteWalk` is consumed by every quote-walking function in the file, and a test asserts the toggle exists in exactly one place. *(2)* A **here-string** carrying a secret VALUE was cut out of the text before tokenizing and handed to the `history` rule alone, so `cat <<<"$SECRET"`, `base64 <<<"$SECRET"`, `tr -d "\n" <<<"$SECRET"` and `cat <<<"$SECRET" >> /var/log/x.log` were all green; it now goes through the same predicate an argument does, and a known stdin **sink** (`sha256sum <<<…`) stays green. The `<<<` is **located on that same walk** rather than by a regex over the raw text, so a quoted string that merely SPELLS a here-string is not read as one. *(3)* One finding's message named the **wrong direction** — `printf … | tee <secret store>` said `tee` *puts the contents of* that path *on stdout* when `tee` **writes** it — so the line keeps its red on the tail message and the unit test that pinned the false sentence was corrected. **Five disclosed bounds that had no test now have one** — the four above plus the one-level depth limit on a secret-FILE read inside a nested substitution, joining the heredoc case that already had one — each pinning CURRENT behaviour, so closing a bound reds the suite instead of leaving the disclosure stale.
  - ⚠ **A fifth round found that sentence was a claim about a SET and the set was not five: ELEVEN of the nineteen bounds the tool disclosed had no pin at all** (a command PREFIX that takes an operand, bare `env` / `printenv`, `declare -p` and friends with no operand, a stdin-SINK whose stdout is its stdin in another mode, `tee` outside a secret-carrying pipeline, a four-space indented block, functions, loops, `eval`, aliases, and a value routed through several variables). **The repair is a guard, not three corrected numbers:** every bound is now tagged `BOUND(<slug>)` in the tool's own header, the suite maps each slug to the test that pins it, and one test asserts the two sets are EQUAL IN BOTH DIRECTIONS — so a bound disclosed with no pin reds, and so does a pin whose tag was deleted. Every disclosed bound gained a pin, and what is checked is the SET rather than a count — the number that stood here was falsified by the very next round, which disclosed another bound. Two of that round's bounds were FALSE POSITIVES pinned in that direction on purpose (`timeout 5 sha256sum` reds naming a command `5`; `tee <secret store>` alone names the wrong direction).
  - ⚠ **A twentieth bound is newly disclosed and deliberately not closed** — a pipeline whose TAIL is a group command `{ …; }` or a subshell `( … )` loses the pipeline mark, so `echo "$SECRET" | { base64; }` is green where `| base64` reds, and `|&` reds naming a command `&`; measured at **0** occurrences over the 1,973 tag-file pairs, so it is disclosed rather than widened.
  - ⛔ **And one defect in the tool's OWN OUTPUT, a member of the class it exists to catch: the `history` finding quoted the literal payload into the CI log**, republishing the value the rule keeps off a readable surface into an Actions log that outlives the branch. It now names the literal by **position and length** and never by value (`argument 2, a literal 16 characters long`). **The verdict is unchanged** — same lines, same rule, same surfaces; only the message text moves, and the census re-derives byte-identical at 399 findings / 5 distinct messages. **DL-321 declined exactly this lint on exactly that ground and the objection is not overturned** — what reversed the decline is an operator ruling on a measurement: all five **fence-resident** members of the class were caught **5/5**, with **zero false positives over 1,973 tag-file pairs** (every `*.md` at all **85** tags in this repo's history produced 399 findings and only five distinct messages, each a known member). **That measurement now ships as an instrument rather than as a retyped figure** — `python3 bin/doc-fence-census.py` re-derives all four numbers from the tag history and prints each distinct message with an example, which is how the "each is a known member" claim is checked rather than taken; it is deliberately **not** a CI job, because a depth-1 checkout has no tags and would measure an empty history while exiting 0.
  - ⚠ **A sixth round stopped patching and fixed the instrument, because the finding count had stopped falling** — rounds three, four, five and six each returned two mechanism findings against a subject the round before had called clean, which is the signal that the review LOOP is at fault rather than the work. The fifth round's guard compares a set of tag NAMES with a set of pin NAMES, so both stay complete while a disclosure describes behaviour the tool does not have. **Each pin now carries its PAYLOADS AND EXPECTED VERDICTS as data** — 21 bounds, 69 payloads, 41 green and 28 red — and one test re-runs every payload through the real walk and asserts the verdict, while a second reads DIRECTION off the tool's own markers and requires a payload in every direction a disclosure names. **The bound that defect was hiding: `BOUND(heredoc)` was disclosed in the wrong direction** — only a prose-shaped body reds as `argv`, while `token=$SECRET`, `export TOKEN=$SECRET` and a bare `$SECRET` inside `<<EOF`, `<<-EOF` or `<<EOF | base64` are all SILENTLY green; both arms are now stated and both carry payloads. ⚠ **A twenty-first bound is disclosed and deliberately not closed:** in a prompt-style fence (`console`, `shell-session`, `bash-session`, `sh-session`, `shellsession`) a ROOT-prompt line is emptied by the comment strip before any rule reads it, so `# echo "$BRIDGE_WEBHOOK_SECRET"` is green under `argv`, `stdout`, `probe`, `log` and `history` where the `$ ` spelling of the same line reds — the `#` arm of the prompt-strip regex was unreachable and is deleted, which changes **no verdict on any payload, any doc here or any tag**. **Nothing in this round changes what CI rejects**; closing either the root-prompt bound or adding `.env` to the secret-path markers would, and both are raised as operator asks rather than taken. The census re-derives byte-identical (85 tags / 1,973 tag-file pairs / 399 findings / 5 distinct messages).
  - ⚠ **`.env` now counts as a secret store, which is the second of that round's two operator asks and is now ruled** — **this changes what CI rejects**, in the loud direction and on **three arms**: `cat .env` reds `stdout`, `curl -H "… $(cat .env)"` reds `argv`, and a command-line LITERAL written into the file (`echo "DB_PASSWORD=…" >> .env`) reds `history` — all green before, because `CLAUDE.md` §3 puts the DB password in the Laravel `.env` and a runbook that `cat`s one resolves a secret onto stdout exactly as a `cat` of the channel-token file does. Measured before the ask and again on the change: **0 findings over the 29 markdown files here, identical before and after**, and watched failing both ways — deleting the marker reds 8 assertions, widening it to a bare `env` reds the control that says the DOT is what fires. ⚠ **One false-positive arm ships with it, disclosed as `BOUND(env-template)` rather than narrowed:** `.env` is a substring, so ANY path text containing it matches (`.envrc`, `.env.dist`, `conf.environment`, …) and the shipped template `.env.example` is the one that matters here — a READER on it reds naming a store that holds only placeholders — POSITION decides, and `cp .env.example .env`, the line the install runbooks actually contain, stays green. Narrowing the marker would change what CI *accepts* and is left to its own ruling. The other ask of that round — closing the root-prompt bound — is **still not taken**; its `history` leg, claimed green in the disclosure and pinned nowhere, now carries payloads in both directions.
  - ⚠ **One known member is out of reach and the figure is bounded by it:** DL-321's third site — the *"Compare both values"* row of `docs/multi-host.md`'s failure-mode table — was **prose in a table**, not a fence. **Explanatory prose is never read**, which is the constraint the design turns on: this repo QUOTES the bad forms to teach why they were wrong (`CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md`'s smoke-test preamble quotes DL-322's argv leak), and a lint that reddened on the documentation of a defect would get the explanation deleted — so only fences whose info string names a shell are parsed, and the same bytes are clean in inline backticks and red in a fence. **Proven failable before it was trusted:** `bin/testdata/v0.79.0-live-members.md.fixture` carries all five members verbatim out of the **v0.79.0** tag, each pinned to its line and its rule, with a control that disables each rule in turn and requires that rule's member to disappear — plus a second control that empties the secret vocabulary and requires the whole fixture to go clean. The **green** direction is asserted on this checkout's real markdown, not on a copy of it.
  - ⚠ **The workflow deliberately carries NO `paths:` filter** — a filter is a static enumeration that makes a gate go ABSENT rather than red when it stops covering a file (this repo has paid for that once, card#5977), which would be the wrong shape for a backstop against a class that exists because enumerations are one item behind; the cost is one ~15-second stdlib-python job per PR. If a doc must show a bad form inside an executable fence, waive the line with `# doc-fence-lint: allow <reason>` — a reasonless waiver is reported **and does not suppress**.
  - ⛔ **This does not close the class.** It reads markdown shell fences and nothing else; a member minted in a `.php`, a `.sh`, a workflow `run:` block, a hook script or prose is out of reach by construction. The harness-level controls that would cover the instruments a spelling list cannot — a PostToolUse redactor, a PreToolUse deny — change what the **harness** refuses and stay on **card#8351** for their own ruling.
- **card#8530** — **both structural enforcers of the belongs-to-mapped-board boundary now derive ONE whole-`app/` population, and the post-read one dispositions each site instead of globbing two directories and disclosing the rest as a bound.** **Tests and docs only — no runtime change**; `app/` is touched in two docblocks that had gone stale, and no `app/` behaviour, config key, `.env`, migration or receiver accept/reject moves. The pre-read enforcer (card#8440, DL-330) walked all of `app/` on PHP's tokenizer; the post-read one (DL-292, widened by DL-301) walked `app/Bridge/Handlers/Kanban*Handler.php` + `app/Console/Commands/Bridge/*.php` and carried a **STATED BOUND** for "a writer anywhere else in `app/`". ⛔ **That bound was a declaration with no check, and it was already false when it was written:** `BoardCreateCardTool::placement()` reads a card's `board_id` and compares it to the configured board outside both globs, and it was the *pre-read* class that found it — because that one derived the tree. Two halves of one boundary deriving two populations is how a site is covered by neither: a new `app/Bridge/Tools/` method that resolves an id board-scoped (green on the pre-read list) and then hand-writes `$card['board_id'] !== $cfg->boardId` to decide a WRITE was invisible to both. **The derivation is hoisted into one shared test primitive** (`Tests\Support\SourceScan::sitesInApp`) that both classes call — the tokenized walk, the whole-`app/` file population and its floor, and the `<path>::<function>#<ordinal>` keying, spelled once. ⛔ **The glob was NOT widened onto `app/Bridge/Tools/`, which DL-302 had already rejected on the merits** — that layer's axis is `BoardToolsConfig`, not `WritebackMapping`, so routing its compares through `MappedBoardGuard::refuses()` would mint a writeback refusal alert, with its dedup tuple and its outcome, for a call that has no repo and, on three of the four sites, SUCCEEDED. A wider glob with an empty expectation buys one exemption and no coverage, and its false reds push authors to launder non-refusal compares through the refusal primitive. **The post-read leg now enumerates every site in `app/` naming `board_id` or `card_not_on_mapped_board`** — with an array-literal KEY (`'board_id' => …`, a field being written) excluded by construction and a partial spelling (`configured_board_id`, `q=board_id={$id}`) not being the name — and holds set equality against a list that **dispositions** each one, naming the SUBJECT it reads: the primitive's own sites (the `REASON` constant, both halves of the `belongs()` predicate, the `boardContext()` pair); the board-tools door — `BoardCreateCardTool::placement()` (a report-only warning on a create that SUCCEEDED, DL-299, plus an entry that is the field NAME used as a label, not a read), `BoardMyCardsTool::observedBoard()` (report-only on a read path, DL-302) and `BoardCorrectCardTool::matchingRow()` (the ownership re-establishment on board-scoped search rows, whose refusal is deliberately non-disclosing, DL-326); and the sites that read a config block, a webhook envelope or a response header rather than a card at all. **The list itself is the only place any count of it is true** — `WritebackRefusalSignalCoverageTest`'s `DISPOSITIONED`, which the test holds set equality against on every run; a figure restated here would be a second copy with no guard (card#8555). **Seen to fail four ways:** a scratch `app/Bridge/Tools/ScratchTenantCompareTool.php` with a hand-written board compare gating a write reds the new leg (`Bridge/Tools/ScratchTenantCompareTool.php::correct#1`) and was **GREEN** on the old one; the same scratch MOVED to `app/Bridge/Handlers/` reds the new leg and was **GREEN on the old one too** — the old leg's handler population was the `Kanban*Handler.php` NAME glob, so an off-pattern handler was outside it as well (the bound DL-292 stated, measured rather than assumed); renamed `KanbanScratchTenantCompareHandler.php` it reds both, which is the one arm the old leg could see; deleting `placement#1`'s disposition reds naming it; and the pre-read class's own two fixture controls (9 guard shapes + the ordinal/file-scope attribution) pin the hoisted walk unchanged. ⛔ **A green run still says only that every such site is accounted for** — `tests/` is out of the population, an array-literal key is out by construction, and a disposition that names a config block is taken on the reviewer's word exactly as the pre-read class's board-scoped arm is. ⛔ **AND THE RULE THIS MINTS IS NOT A DESCRIPTION OF THE TREE:** census instruments over `app/` remain that carry their own copy of the walk — one of them a byte-for-byte duplicate of the hoisted file population, in the same directory — so `CLAUDE_TESTING.md` names them as an **un-migrated remainder** with the recipe that re-derives it, rather than a hand-written member list that told the next author the class they were about to copy was out of scope. **card#8575 owns that consolidation**; migrating them is out of scope here. The shared walk's stated exclusion (the scope-tracking tokens never reach the caller's predicate) also stops being a declaration with no check: a control asserts it on a fixture, with a presence witness beside the absence, and was seen to red under two separate mutations of the dispatch.
- **card#8440 (DL-330)** — **the pre-read tenant check on the kanban client's by-id read is no longer prose-only: a seventh `getCard()` call site that resolves an author-supplied id unguarded now REDS.** **No runtime change, no config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change, and the six existing call sites are byte-identical** — what is new is `tests/Feature/Writeback/GetCardTenantCheckCoverageTest.php`, an allowlist over every `->getCard(` call in `app/`. Until it landed, the rule DL-323 and DL-330 both state was carried by three docblocks and landed **by hand** at one call site each on card#8375 and card#8415 — a declaration with no check, which is how the N+1th copy mints the bug again (here a cross-tenant read of a foreign card id **before** the 403 wall, card#7846's shape). The post-read compare has had its structural enforcer since DL-292; this is its pre-read twin. **The population is re-derived every run** on PHP's own tokenizer (so a docblock `{@see …}`, a comment and the declaration itself are excluded by construction) and keyed `<path under app/>::<function>#<ordinal>`, never by line number. ⭐ **The two arms are not equally checkable and the class says so:** a site declaring the guard is **verified for control flow** — an honoured `MappedBoardGuard::refusesCardIdOutsideMappedBoard()` must precede it in the same body, where *honoured* means an `if` whose body exits at its top level (`return`/`throw`/`continue`/`break`), because a guard whose refusal is dropped on the floor reads exactly like one that works; a site declaring board-scoped id resolution is **declared**, with the producing read named per entry; **id correspondence between guard and read is checked on neither arm** (a stated bound, not a gap found live). The **partition** is machine-checked in both directions, so the unverified list cannot absorb a checkable site. ⚠ **A contributor adding a by-id read must now answer which mechanism scopes it** — that is the point, and the red says so with both remediations. **Seen to fail four ways before it was trusted:** a scratch handler with a bare `$client->getCard($authorId)`; the shipped `kanban_move_card` guard with its `return;` deleted; the guard added to a board-scoped site; and a top-level closure with no enclosing method, which the first draft skipped (the one shape that failed open — R1 review) and which is now keyed to the file scope. ⛔ **A green run says every by-id read in `app/` declares a mechanism — never that no cross-tenant read is possible:** `tests/` is out of the population, only the shipped `if (…) { <exit>; }` shape counts as honoured (a new spelling is a review event, not a scanner to widen), and control flow inside a function is not modelled.

- **card#8378 (DL-326)** — **`board_correct_card`: an impl seat can CORRECT a card it filed, instead of minting a second card to say the first one is wrong.** ⚠ **ADDS A WRITE SURFACE — it changes what the board-tools door accepts** (operator-gated on the card before implementation). No migration, no config key, no `.env` change, and nothing the receiver accepts or rejects moves; ⚠ **but the writeback user's board role now needs `task.update` — new for this door.** A PATCH carrying anything other than `workflow_stage_id` alone authorizes `update`, not `move` (kanban DL-204 → `TaskPolicy::update` → `BoardPermissions::TASK_UPDATE`, an independently grantable `board_custom` slot), and a correction always carries `name`/`description`/`tags`; the two existing board tools need only `board.view` and `task.create`. A **Member**-role writeback user already holds it; a narrowed custom role gets a permanent 403 on every correction, named as an install fault. Sanctum token abilities are unchanged (`write`). Grant list: `docs/writeback.md` § 1 owns it — the one place the set is stated; `docs/config-schema.md`'s writeback-token row and `docs/kanban-integration-contract.md` §2 POINT at it rather than restating it, so the copies cannot drift; an install that never enables `board_tools` is unaffected (the registry stays inert without a per-agent block, DL-217). **The gap:** a seat's whole board surface was **create + read**, so duplicate-minting was the only available response to its own wrong card — and duplicates defeat every downstream instrument that keys on one card per subject. `kbcard patch` is not the answer and is not offered as one: it is a local CLI needing a board API token a seat does not hold. **⭐ Scope: you may correct what you MINTED.** The discriminator already existed — the `created-by:<agent>` tag `board_create_card` stamps, which a caller cannot forge (the prefix is reserved on create and the guard casefolds) and which is the **only per-seat provenance a card carries**: kanban's `actor_type: service` covers every CLI writer and `actor_id` names the shared writeback USER, so neither can say *which seat filed this*. The compare is **case-sensitive** (agent names are filesystem-cased, so `me` and `ME` can be two seats). **Two independent narrowings, both required:** the id is established through the **board-scoped** `GET /tasks/search.json?q=board_id=<yours> id=<card>` (card#8375/DL-323's primitive — a seat's `card_id` is caller-supplied against a GLOBAL id space) and the verdict is read off the **rows**, never the call; `getCard()` is never used. **The LANE is deliberately not checked** — a human may re-lane a card legitimately, and a lane test would make a re-laned card permanently uncorrectable by the seat that filed it. **Correctable: `name`, `description`, `tags` — and nothing else.** ⛔ The offered set is deliberately **narrower than `kbcard patch`'s corrective setters**: `type`, `external_id` and `origin` are refused too, because **this tool never writes a field `board_create_card` would refuse at birth** — a wider correction authority is a laundering route around the create guards. Every field a caller might name is refused **by name with its owner** (column moves are a different authority; the writeback owns payload/correlation refs; `block_reason` is DL-193's; a retire is a lifecycle act), and anything else is refused as an unknown argument — **nothing is silently ignored**, which is the "refuse loudly, never no-op" this card was filed on. ⚠ **`tags` is replaced WHOLESALE by kanban, so the write re-sends every tag on the card that is somebody ELSE'S — a set deliberately WIDER than the tags you may not send.** Two halves: the ones you could not restore because you may not supply them (`created-by:` — your mint stamp, `idem:` — your idempotency key, `type:`/`triaged` — the triage pass's work), and **the holds you MAY supply and may not drop** — `no-automove` (the writeback's all-outcome pin, whose other half `block_reason` this tool already refuses by name) plus every **`hold_marker_tags`** value your install declares for that board (DL-194), read board-keyed through the new `WritebackConfig::holdMarkerTagsForBoard()`. Deriving the preserve set from the refuse set is the defect that was caught in review: it dropped a HUMAN'S PIN on a `tags` correction and handed the next `merged` event the terminal move card#8289 exists to prevent. ⛔ **A `writeback.json` the bridge cannot parse REFUSES a `tags` correction** rather than reading as "no holds declared" (`name`/`description` are unaffected); no `writeback.json` at all is a different and true answer — no declared holds. **One argument rule: a PRESENT key is a correction, an ABSENT key leaves its field alone** — so `tags: []` means *drop my tags* and an empty `description` clears the body; `null` and `""` are one value because Laravel's global `ConvertEmptyStringsToNull` rewrites `""` before the HTTP controller reads `args` while the ssh door preserves it, and one contract on both transports is worth more than the distinction. ⚠ **A 401/403/404/422 the BOARD answers is reported as a 422 refusal, not the retryable 502** — every one fails identically however often you send it (a rotated token, an ability the token lacks, a card that stopped existing, a value kanban's validator rejects), and the message says when the cause is an install fault rather than your arguments; a 5xx still answers 502, pinned by a control. The 422 arm never echoes the board's response body. ⛔ **The 403 message names the token's ABILITIES, not board membership**: kanban's search floors a caller to its member boards and answers 200-with-zero-rows, so a membership gap can never produce a 403 — it surfaces as the *not one of yours* refusal, which now names it (an unreadable board and an empty one are one answer here, mirroring DL-323). **⚠ NEW BOUNDS, mirrored from kanban's own rules** (`name` ≤ 255, each tag ≤ 64 — `App\Support\TaskWriteRules`, held in one place as `KanbanFieldLimits` and stated as a mirror): an over-long value is refused BY NAME instead of arriving as a board 422 the seat reads as retryable. **The tag cap is shared with `board_create_card`, so that tool's answer to an over-long tag changes from `502 upstream board error` to a `422` naming the cap** — kanban rejected the create either way; what changes is the status a consumer sees. **`description` is trimmed**, so a whitespace-only body CLEARS on the ssh door exactly as it already did on HTTP (same call, one meaning, on both transports). **Client:** one new general `KanbanClient::patchCard($cardId, $fields)` — the flat-body PATCH primitive `moveCard`, `setBlockReason` and `stampCorrelationRefs` now delegate to, wire-identical (three byte-identical bodies were the primitive waiting to be named). **Channel server snapshot 0.9.8 → 0.9.9** (the new tool is advertised there, and a tool absent from `TOOL_DEFINITIONS` is unreachable from a seat). Each of the nine guards was watched **RED against a mutant** before it was trusted. ⛔ **The client consolidation silently narrowed ANOTHER guard's population, and that guard caught it** — `WritebackSuccessBoardRecordTest` derives the client's write methods by finding `->patch(`/`->post(`/`->delete(` in a method body, so the moment the three narrow verbs delegated to `patchCard` they stopped matching and **nine accounted call sites left the card#7212 write census with every behavioural test still green**. The derivation now follows `$this-><method>(` to a fixed point (a control fixture pins that it declines a method delegating to a READ), so it describes what a write IS rather than how one is spelled.

- **card#8374 (DL-331)** — **`bridge:check`'s retention posture now reports what the store is HOLDING, not only what the config says.** ⚠ **Changes what the command reports as a problem**; **no exit code moves in either direction** (`warn` has never moved one), the registered check set is unchanged at **38**, no check id is added, the inventory counts are unchanged and `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. No migration, no config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change. **The defect.** `retention: on (delete >30d + null payloads >7d, …)` is a restatement of `config/bridge.php` and nothing else, so it reads identically on an install holding four rows and on the one that produced the ask — **894 MB of a 1.2 GB store was 30 days of full GitHub payloads**, under a retention that was working correctly the whole time and was discoverable only by running `SUM(LENGTH(payload))` unprompted. The same green line is also printed by an install whose payload-nulling leg is switched OFF, so `on` was true and useless. **What is added, as three legs answering three separate questions.** (1) A **store line** — the database's size as the engine reports it, the payload bytes inside it and their share, and the oldest retained row's age **against the configured delete window**; `ok` inside the window, **`warn`** past it, naming `retention.batch` because a backlog draining at that many rows per delivery is the benign reading and must be distinguishable from a delete leg that is not running. (2) An empty `retention.older_than` is its own **`warn`**: rows and their dispatches are never deleted. (3) An empty `retention.null_payloads_older_than` is a **`warn` carrying the bytes** — *"11987 of 12345 retained rows still carry a full webhook payload, holding 894.0 MiB … kept until the 30d row-delete window removes the row"* — the line the reporting install never got. Legs 2 and 3 are mutually exclusive by construction; both windows empty is the pre-existing `MISCONFIGURED` arm. **No `fail` and no new `Severity` case.** ⛔ **A figure the database driver cannot source is ABSENT from the line, never inferred and never a zero** (DL-306's ruling, applied here): the size and the payload byte count need engine-specific SQL — measured, not assumed, that `length()` counts **bytes** on MariaDB and **characters** on SQLite (`{"x":"é"}` is 10 bytes and 9 characters, and the test pinning it was watched red against the naive spelling) — and there is no cross-engine size query at all, so an unknown driver gets the portable half and the line says so in words. A store that could not be read reports **`unvalidated`**, explicitly not an empty one — and ⛔ **the two OFF-leg warnings are emitted ONLY on a run that could measure the store**, because the counts and bytes ARE those warnings; a `bridge:check` whose store read failed is therefore not evidence that the retention legs are on, and says so. ⛔ **THE PAYLOAD SHARE IS NOT PRINTED ON MariaDB AT ALL, and that is a measurement, not a precaution.** The share is a quotient across two accounting bases — a live `sum(length(payload))` over this app's rows, over whatever the ENGINE reports for the database. Only SQLite makes the first a subset of the second by construction (`page_count * page_size` is the whole file). On MariaDB **10.6.28 and 11.8.9 alike**, 200 rows carrying **13107200 bytes** of payload (64 KiB each, above the InnoDB inline-row limit) left `webhook_events.data_length` at **16384 — one page** — and `index_length` unchanged, while `table_rows` refreshed **0 → 200** and `avg_row_length` became **81**: the statistics were not stale, `ROW_FORMAT=Dynamic` simply stores those bytes OFF-PAGE and neither figure counts them. The whole schema reported 245760 bytes against 13107200 of payload. ⚠ **The failure that decided this is the PLAUSIBLE one, not an obvious `~234% of the database`:** an install whose payloads STRADDLE the inline limit gets a believable percentage over a denominator holding only part of its own numerator, on a line whose whole purpose is a capacity decision, with nothing to flag it. The probe now DECLARES whether the two figures are on one basis and the check withholds the share in words where they are not — the clause states the basis mismatch and sends the operator to the TABLE's own tablespace file (`webhook_events.ibd` under the default `innodb_file_per_table=ON`) rather than to the whole data directory, which is a third measurement again; it is printed by `App\Bridge\Check\Checks\RetentionPostureCheck::payloadShare()` and deliberately not quoted here; a share above 100% is refused independently, whatever a probe declares. Both byte figures still print — only their ratio is withheld. The privilege-free alternatives were closed by measurement too: `information_schema.innodb_tablespaces` does not exist on either version and `innodb_sys_tablespaces` answers **`1227 Access denied; you need (at least one of) the PROCESS privilege(s)`** to an ordinary schema user, so adopting it would make the leg unrunnable on a least-privilege install; `avg_row_length * table_rows` is 81 x 200 = 16200, i.e. `data_length` again. ⚠ **`bridge:check` now costs one full scan of `webhook_events`**, seconds on a multi-GB store; the receiver's own retention pass is untouched and never runs it. ⚠ **The measurement sits behind a `RetentionStoreProbe` seam pinned by the golden harness**, because its subject is the storage ENGINE and the suite runs against SQLite and the MariaDB 10.6/11 matrix — a live size read is a different number per job, and unlike a path or a uid a size ENCODES the verdict, so DL-242's rule forbids normalizing it away. The consequence is stated rather than left implicit: **the byte-identical corpus is no evidence that the real SQL parses**, which is what `DatabaseRetentionStoreProbeTest` is for and why the cross-engine claim is made by the MariaDB matrix running it. 39 of 41 golden fixtures gain a line (the two that do not are `retention-disabled` and `retention-misconfigured`, which return before the cost legs), plus 4 new fixtures — the empty, past-window and unmeasurable shapes, and `retention-store-off-page-accounting`, the shape every MariaDB install gets.

- **card#8377 (DL-328)** — **an upstream PR retitle now corrects the dependabot card it silently invalidated — and only where the name is still the bridge's.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK WRITES** (operator-approved): the writeback gains its first card-`name` write after birth. Dependabot retitles its PR *in place* when it retargets a bump, so a card named once at create time is left permanently asserting a version that never shipped — measured on a peer board as **2 cards asserting dependency versions that never shipped**, one of them contradicting another card on the same board, with the correct title arriving on the merge event and being discarded (roundtable 212). `GitHubPrCardMoveClassifier` now emits a MOVE-LESS `kanban_dependabot_card` target (outcome `renamed`) on a `pull_request.edited` that changed a dependabot PR's TITLE, carrying both the new title and GitHub's `changes.title.from`; `KanbanDependabotCardHandler` restamps the correlated card's name through the new `KanbanClient::patchCard($id, $fields)` verb — **gated on the card's current name being byte-identical to that previous title**, which is exactly the string the bridge stamped when it minted the card. **A card renamed by a human is left alone** (a paired control test asserts it, and reds under the naive "always overwrite from the upstream title" fix); nothing reads the shape of a name, nothing assumes the version moved forward (the observed drift runs both ways), and nothing derives the name from the head branch ref, which is frozen at branch creation while the diff is retargeted. No migration, no new or changed config key, no `.env` change, no webhook-subscription change (`pull_request` was already subscribed whole), and no new board custom field. **⚠ UPGRADING — one board PERMISSION, not a config change: the writeback token needs kanban's `task.update` on the mapped boards.** A `{name}` PATCH is not a stage-only PATCH, so kanban authorizes it as `update` rather than `move` (kanban DL-204), and this arm's other writes are create / stage-only move / `_action: archive` — three separately grantable abilities — so the restamp is the FIRST write on the dependabot arm to take `task.update`. A **Member**-role token already carries it and needs nothing; a **custom**-role token granted move/create without `update` gets a permanent 403 on every retitle, reported as `dependabot_card_4xx` (loud, never a silent skip). An install already running the draft `block_reason` overlay or the `payload` correlation stamp is likewise unaffected — both already took that ability on their own arms. The full permission set the writeback needs is now stated in `docs/writeback.md` § *A least-privilege writeback token* (it said "card moves", which understated it), declared as condition (3) on `docs/kanban-integration-contract.md` §2's PATCH row, and owned by **DL-328 bound (e)** — which also records that this **supersedes DL-187's** "moving a card and writing its payload are the SAME endpoint/permission/ability", true when written and falsified five days later by kanban's DL-204. **On a create race** (>1 card correlated to one PR) **every card whose name still matches is restamped, and none is collapsed** — both twins carry the same bridge-stamped name, and retiring a duplicate stays the move path's job. **Bound:** the evidence lives on the rename event, so a retitle the bridge never received is NOT repaired here — already-wrong cards are a separate one-off backfill. Every other `edited` action stays the no-op it was.

### Changed

- **card#8344 (DL-327)** — **a PR can now declare that it CITES a card it does not close, and the writeback honours it: `[no-close]` in the PR title withholds the merge move.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK ACTS ON** (operator-approved). No migration, no config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change, and **`pr-title-lint`'s accept-set is byte-unchanged** — the marker was already accepted there; what moved is its prose: the step comment, the skip line and the exit-1 remediation message. **The defect is the last of four doors into one terminal move.** Since DL-308 a merge closes a card **structurally** whenever the head ref names it, which is what `board-card-start` mints — so a PR written *for* a card while deliberately not finishing it (a design note, a reference doc, a partial spike on the card's branch) carries **precisely that ref** and promoted the card into a terminal stage on merge, asserting work landed that did not. Measured twice on real merges (`mezzanine#24` → card 7341, roundtable 388; the shape again in card#7348's population); under-promotion is recoverable by hand, this is not, and the release sweep propagates it. ⛔ **No predicate over the artifact could have closed it** — card#8294 (the strict token), card#8306 (the revert) and card#8289 (the pin) each read the ARTIFACT, and a context PR is indistinguishable from a closing one on the branch, the title and the diff. *"I am citing this card, not closing it"* is a fact about the author. ⛔ **And the card-side pin does not cover it, which is what roundtable 388 asked**: `PinGuard` is **pre-emptive** and lives on the CARD, so it helps only where somebody knew in advance that a future PR would cite that card — in the measured incident nobody did, and it was caught weeks later. (That pin's own gap is separately fixed and released in **v0.79.0**, card#8289.) **The fix reuses the marker CI already accepts and mints no second vocabulary:** a new `NoCloseGrammar` owns the literal `[no-close]`, and the two authorities that own the closure routes consult it — `ClosureGrammar` returns the empty set at its single choke point (so `closedCardIds()`, `closedDls()` and everything derived refuse together) and `PrOutcome::mergeClosesCard()` gains it as a conjunct — so the event path and `bridge:reconcile` get the ruling from the same place and cannot disagree (the DL-305 §6 lockstep, third application). It is read off the **event payload's title**, which both paths already carry: **no new API call, no new token scope**. ⛔ **It SUBTRACTS and never ADDS, which is why it is not the accept-surface DL-318 refused:** that entry declined a `[close-anyway]` marker because its effect would be to MOVE a card the writeback had refused to move; `[no-close]` can only WITHHOLD — it cannot select a card, authorize a stage or overturn a guard, so a planted marker achieves nothing but a card that stands still, loudly logged. ⛔ **It is a LITERAL, not a grammar** — `no close intended`, `[noclose]` and `(no-close)` are prose and still close the card; case and position are free. ⭐ **Precedence with the pin: BOTH hold, neither overrides** — the marker is read at classify time (no move target is emitted at all, so the handler where `PinGuard` lives is never reached), the pin at write time on every outcome; either alone withholds, and there is no order in which two withholdings disagree. ⛔ **Correlation is untouched:** the card is still selected, `opened` / `started` / `closed_unmerged` still fire, the PR refs are still stamped, and the backstop still sees the card — deleting the marker is the whole recovery. ⚠ **Both operator-facing lines gain a marker arm** (the classifier's withheld-merge warning and `bridge:reconcile`'s skip line), because the default sentence is FALSE about a marked PR — it says *the head branch ref does not name the card*, which is normally untrue here, and would send an operator to rename a branch to undo a refusal they asked for; the non-marked lines are byte-identical. `PrOutcome::describeClosure()` / `describeClosureAccepted()` gain a derived subtractive clause for the same reason. ⚠ **A quotation is not an assertion** — a `[no-close]` inside a quoted revert title is the ORIGINAL author's and does not veto this author's own closing form (DL-318's ruling re-derived, not assumed). ⚠ **A title that merely MENTIONS the marker is marked by it** — a literal has no quoting rule, so a docs PR about this feature declares a non-closure by talking about one; disclosed rather than repaired (the cure is a context rule, which is the grammar this refuses to be), and the direction is under-promotion. ⚠ **One literal, two engines:** `NoCloseGrammar::MARKER` owns the spelling and `PrTitleLintTest` ties the workflow's `optout=` to it — the string **and** the answer sets, comparing the step's real `tr | grep -F` against `stripos()` over a corpus that splits. ⚠ **The superseded claim is swept by PATTERN, not by file list, and the count is deliberately not stated** (the first sweep enumerated files and left standing the copy an author actually reads — the step's exit-1 message): every hit of `nothing at runtime` / `suppresses this check` / `CI-only` / `inert at runtime` / `no part of the bridge` across `*.php *.md *.yml *.sh` is corrected or dispositioned — including the two RELEASED rows below (v0.80.0's DL-318 row and v0.79.0's card#8294 row), which keep their text and gain a one-clause `[superseded by DL-327]` pointer. Corrected where they stood — `RevertGrammar`'s and the workflow comment's *"a CI-only suppression that nothing at runtime reads"* (the `[close-anyway]` ruling they supported is untouched), `CLAUDE_CONVENTIONS.md` § *PR titles*, and the `writeback.json` rows in `docs/config-schema.md` and `docs/kanban-integration-contract.md`, each of which enumerated the two closure routes as a COMPLETE sufficient condition the marker falsifies. ⭐ **The last two are DELETED-AND-POINTED, not re-synced** — each is now one sentence plus the pointer it already carried, leaving `docs/writeback.md` § *Mention vs closure* the single prose owner of the route list; the one copy that stays inline is `pr-title-lint.yml`'s bash, which a program loads and which is GUARDED by `PrTitleLintTest`'s answer-set ties rather than deleted. ⚠ **One of them is a TEST docblock whose STATED scope was wider than its predicate, and it was already false before this change:** `PrTitleLintTest`'s whole-step tie claimed its biconditional covered *"the only [predicate] a title and a branch can decide"*, when card#8306's revert refusal already made the gate and the writeback answer differently on a title-decidable input (measured: `Revert "feat: … (closes card#4811)"` on an ordinary branch — gate rc 0, writeback refuses). Both families are now pinned with one-variable controls. ⭐ **The DIRECTION is stated at the strength it holds and MEASURED rather than asserted:** every disagreement either grammar's own RULING creates — the marker, the revert — has the gate passing and the writeback withholding (under-promotion, repaired by moving the card), and the one known reverse is a PRICE, not a gap: DL-272's enumerated separator class (`closes<TAB>card#N`) reds the gate on a merge the writeback moves. Both directions, each family with its control, are enumerated and driven through the real extracted bash step and the real predicates by `PrTitleLintTest::test_the_enumerated_disagreement_families_point_the_direction_they_declare()`, which fails with the row printed on any row that answers other than the direction its family declares — the prose above no longer holds the claim, it points at that leg. ⚠ **The exit-1 remediation message is corrected too and now has a test that READS it** — it told the author the marker *"suppresses THIS CHECK and nothing else"*, and nothing in the suite looked at the string. Plus **additive annotations on DL-318 and DL-308**, whose entries stay frozen. ⚠ **One cross-repo route is now DECLARED** (DL-327 § *Bounds*): `release-promote-cards.yml` hands the toolkit's `promote` action this release's DLs, and without its `shipped-stage-ids` input that tool would promote a matched card to Released from ANY stage — including one the marker deliberately kept out of Shipped. The input's presence and shape are now asserted (`ReleaseTagGateTest`); its EQUALITY with the mapping's `stages.merged` is declared and unchecked, because that id lives in install config outside this repo. ⚠ **One divergence disclosed rather than repaired**, the same shape the branch and revert predicates already record: the lint tests the raw title and the writeback subtracts the quoted revert wrapper first, so a hand-made revert quoting a marked title is marked to CI and un-marked to the writeback — CI then demands nothing, the direction that cannot produce a wrong move. **Every new witness is paired with a one-variable control** and was watched RED before the wiring landed: a veto is trivially satisfied by a predicate that refuses everything, which is the DL-305 failure mode this area exists to avoid.

### Fixed

- **card#8339 (DL-329)** — **the CHANGELOG gate stopped telling an author to add a SECOND entry when the first one was already standing in a released section.** The release fold renames `## [Unreleased]` to `## [X.Y.Z]` and opens a fresh empty one above it — an **insertion**, so the entries do not move, the heading above them does. A branch cut before the fold merges **cleanly** and lands its entry inside the released section, which then claims work that release did not ship, while the real `[Unreleased]` reads empty. ⛔ **The gate was already RED on this and this is not a correctness escape** — what it could not do was say WHY, and its remedy (*"Fix: add an `[Unreleased]` entry citing `card#NNNN`"*) was the exact opposite of the right move: following it files a duplicate and leaves the released section corrupted. Measured on run 33544122079, where card#8351's entry was standing under `[0.80.0]` while the gate asked for one. **Both failure arms** — tokened and tokenless — now locate the released section carrying a line **this branch introduced** and, **only when that section was cut while the branch was open — proved by requiring the heading to stand on the base and to be ABSENT at the branch's FORK POINT — the parent of the oldest commit on the branch's OWN first-parent line, not its merge-base (which a branch that merges the base absorbs the fold into) and not the oldest commit in the range (which is a sibling's, when the branch merged one), not inferred from the line's position** — name it: *"your entry is under `[X.Y.Z]` because the branch predates the fold — move it under `[Unreleased]`"*, with an explicit *do not write a second entry*. **The predicate has THREE legs, and the third is not a tighter spelling of the first two:** a branch that forks pre-fold, merges the folded base (the remedy's own first instruction) and then corrects a line the fold had already shipped satisfies both history legs while the fold sentence is false of it, and the three trees the gate can read are byte-identical either way — so the gate also requires that **the branch delete no line of that section as it stands on the base**: a fold carries lines in and deletes none, a correction deletes. Other shapes that put a branch line under a released heading fail the predicate and get the message the gate always printed; **DL-329 owns the predicate and is the single place its residual list is kept** — this entry does not restate it. ⚠ **DIAGNOSTIC ONLY: what CI accepts and rejects is byte-for-byte unchanged** — the detection runs only after the verdict is already a failure, every arm still exits 1, and an unestablished diagnosis prints the message that was always printed. **The detection reads the MERGED tree, and the same one the verdict came from:** `actions/checkout` hands a `pull_request` job `refs/pull/<n>/merge` (measured in that run), which is the only reason the defect is visible to CI at all — on the branch alone the entry sits under a heading still spelled `[Unreleased]`. `bin/changelog-section.py` gains a **mode**, not a sibling script (`--locate-added`, sharing the one section-boundary carve `extract` now also calls — two carvings that disagree about where a section ends is DL-276's own defect class); its needles are the branch's new lines from a merge-base `$BASE...$HEAD` diff **minus every line the base already carried**, because every released section here carries a `### Fixed` head and without that subtraction a plain omission would be told it was a misfiling. ⭐ **The control is a real merged tree, not a re-implementation:** the fixture forks, folds the base, adds the entry on the branch, asserts the merge is CLEAN and that the merged file carries the entry inside `## [1.1.0]`, then drives the workflow's real bash over it — plus a **discriminator** (same fork, same fold, branch wrote no entry) that pins the new text is not printed on every failure, and a **green control** that the remedy the message asks for is the one that turns the gate green. **Six extractor mutations, two workflow-arm mutations and eight on the predicate itself** were each watched red and the whole table was re-measured at the final head (DL-329 carries it, including two hand-counted tallies it corrects), and there are **four** discriminators, not one: same fork/same fold/branch wrote no entry; same released-section membership with no fold; the same released heading standing on the base with the branch forked one commit BEFORE it in one fixture and one commit AFTER it in the other; and the same fork, fold and base with the branch CORRECTING the shipped line instead of adding its own. ⚠ **One bound, stated rather than claimed closed** (a second — that the three-dot diff was pinned by no fixture — is now CLOSED: the deletion leg reads the same diff's `-` side, where `..` shows the base's own fold as a deletion, so swapping it reds three fixtures): the step's **scope predicate** still classifies the PR with a two-dot `git diff --name-only`, so on a pre-fold branch it reports files the BASE changed as this PR's — a change to what CI rejects, so it is DISCLOSED at the predicate itself rather than taken here, and it needs its own item — card#8339 closes with this change, so parking it there would abandon it. `VERSIONING.md` § Release flow step 4 now names the relocation every in-flight branch owes after a fold.
- **card#8454 (DL-335)** — **the dependabot handler stops retiring a card a human has parked: its closed-unmerged ARCHIVE and its survivor MOVE now consult `PinGuard`.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK REFUSES** (operator-approved), in the refusing direction only — nothing that was refused before is now written. `PinGuard::isPinned` (DL-178) had exactly three consumers — `KanbanMoveCardHandler` (all-outcome since card#8289), `bridge:reconcile` and the release-promote sweep — and `KanbanDependabotCardHandler` was not among them, so a closed-unmerged dependabot PR **archived** a card carrying a `block_reason` or a `no-automove` tag that both of the other two skipped: card#8289's event-path-vs-backstop asymmetry one handler over, and worse in kind, because an archive takes the card OFF the board where no backstop reconciles it. ⛔ **No override:** the DL-194 unpark and the DL-195 revive are `started` / `reopened` outcomes a dependabot PR never produces. The refusal is the existing shape — a `Log::warning` plus the `pinned_no_automove` `alert_channel` signal, carrying the `card_id` and the PR number. ⚠ **One alert per repo, not per card:** this handler's dedup tuple carries its synthetic `dependabot_card` outcome, so the first pinned card in a repo signals and later ones reach the durable log only. ⚠ **A card pinned through its own close stays live on the board** — GitHub delivers a close once, so nothing retries it; that is the intended reading of a hold, and lifting the pin lets any later event complete a move (correlation is untouched). ⛔ **Two things deliberately UNCHANGED:** the create arm (no card exists yet to carry a pin) and `CardCollapse`'s duplicate-archive, which retires a bridge-minted create-race twin rather than acting on a card's lifecycle and is shared with a caller outside the mapped-board regime. `KanbanCoordCardMoveHandler` remains the one mover that moves a pinned card, recorded in `PinGuard`'s docblock rather than closed here.

### Fixed

- **card#8527 (DL-295 amendment)** — **four PR gates stop pairing the checked-out merge commit with a STALE `github.event.pull_request.base.sha`; the base snapshot is now the merge's own first parent.** ⚠ **THIS CHANGES WHAT CI REJECTS** (operator-approved), and it is CI-only: no app code, no migration, no config key, no route, no `.env` change, nothing the receiver accepts or rejects. **The defect, measured not inferred:** GitHub does not refresh `pull_request.base.sha` on every `synchronize`, so it can LAG the base tip the merge commit for the same event was actually computed against. On PR #640, run 33598959720 (2026-09-02 06:28Z), the event carried `base.sha` `549c894` while the merge commit `642decf` it checked out has first parent `7a11085` — `dev`'s tip once #639 had merged. `dl-collision-gate.yml` paired those two, read `dev`'s own **DL-335** as a number the PR had minted, and **refused an innocent PR** at predicate rc 6 — the exact false refusal the workflow's own comment says the pairing exists to prevent. **DL-295 bound (f) disclosed this shape twelve days earlier**, in the direction it fired, and said nothing in this repo could verify the pairing; that bound is now closed rather than restated. **The fix is one primitive, not four patches:** `bin/pr-base-snapshot.sh` prints `HEAD^1` — the base commit the checked-out merge RECORDS — after asserting HEAD is a two-parent merge whose second parent is the PR head sha the event names, and printing **no sha** with a named reason on either leg failing. **Four call sites move to it:** `dl-collision-gate.yml`, both steps of `changelog-gate.yml`, `channel-server-supply-chain.yml`'s version-bump guard, and `release-artifacts-gate.yml` (a pinned composite action's `base-sha` input, so the derivation lands at the call site and reaches the action through a step output) — ⚠ **that fourth one had NO live red on the PR #640 shape and moves for one derivation, not for a defect:** the action consumes `base-sha` only through `merge-base(base, head)`, which a lagging base cannot move unless the PR branch has itself merged the base. ⛔ **This is NOT the `git merge-base "$BASE" HEAD` "correction" DL-295 rejected, and that rejection stands** — `merge-base` RE-DERIVES a base from whatever head it is handed and gives the FORK POINT to the first caller whose head is not the PR merge; `HEAD^1` derives nothing, it reads what the merge records. Measured: substituting `git merge-base` for the derivation is inert on most of the workflow harness and red on a minority of it — the cases that pin the pairing, and card#8339's pre-fold diagnosis, which reads the base as well — the inertness that made the rewrite look harmless, now with tests that catch it; `CLAUDE_TESTING.md` owns the figure, because its denominator moves with every merge. ⚠ **`changelog-gate.yml`'s two steps also stop diffing against `pull_request.head.sha` and diff the WORK TREE**, because moving only the base end would pair a fresher base with an independently-sourced head — the same defect on the other operand. ⚠ **`changelog-gate.yml` keeps `$HEAD` (the PR BRANCH TIP) as an input**, because card#8339's pre-fold diagnosis asks where the branch STARTED and what the branch ADDS — properties of the branch's own history that the merge commit's first-parent chain does not carry. Only the SCOPE diffs move to the work tree. ⭐ **card#8441 is CLOSED by this derivation and PR #642's three-dot spelling is superseded:** `HEAD^1` is an ancestor of `HEAD`, so `"$BASE...HEAD"` ≡ `"$BASE..HEAD"` — three dots are a no-op on this pairing. Where a branch-tip three-dot range and the merge's delta differ — a branch that made the same edit the base already shipped — the merge's delta is right (nothing lands, no entry owed), pinned and watched red under the three-dot spelling. **Merge delta ⊆ three-dot ⊆ two-dot: newly ACCEPTED are card#8441's two cells (a branch cut before a release fold no longer reads as a release PR; a docs-only branch is no longer pulled in by the base's own `app/` change) and the identical-change one; newly REJECTED is nothing.** DL-329's *"sibling filed, not fixed"* is discharged in place, no DL-337 is minted, and `VERSIONING.md`'s gate table now says what *"the PR changes …"* means. ⚠ **One refusal is genuinely NEW, in the loud direction:** a job standing on something other than this PR's merge commit — a stale `refs/pull/N/merge`, a `push`-triggered run, a re-pointed fixture — now fails with a named reason instead of reporting a verdict about a tree nobody pushed. It cannot make any gate more permissive: the DL collision gate's live-target leg is untouched, and its `added` set can only SHRINK as the base moves forward. ⚑ **The two-parent requirement's obvious worry was MEASURED, not argued** — a PR whose branch has already merged the base: GitHub **never fast-forwards** `refs/pull/<n>/merge`, checked 2026-09-02 against the two open PRs in exactly that state (#635 merge `570d1dc`, #630 merge `6286dc9`), both two-parent while `merge-base --is-ancestor base head` is true. A plain `git merge` in a fixture *does* fast-forward there, so the workflow harnesses pass `--no-ff`. **Pinned:** `DlCollisionGateTest` builds every fixture's work tree as a real merge commit (two parents asserted) and adds the PR #640 divergence with three legs — the step green, the derived base asserted to BE the moved tip and NOT the stale sha, and the predicate run against the stale sha returning **6** and naming the base branch's own entry; plus two cases that the pairing is CHECKED rather than assumed. New `tests/Feature/Workflows/PrBaseSnapshotTest.php` covers the script's own arms (exit 2/3/4/5, empty stdout on every refusal, an octopus merge refused because `HEAD^1` is readable and wrong on three parents) **plus one case pinning the DERIVATION rather than a refusal** — the refusal fixtures are linear, where `HEAD^1` and a fork point are the same commit, so without it the primitive's own harness is inert to the substitution and only the callers' harnesses discriminate. `ChangelogGateTest` gains three forked cells — card#8441's release-step and feature-step ones and the identical-change one — each asserting its own discriminator before the verdict. ⚠ **Cross-repo siblings, reported, NOT fixed here, and NARROWED:** `release-artifacts-gate.yml` on `agent-board-toolkit` and on `kanban-board` pass the same `base-sha` from the same field into the same action; by that action's contract their exposure is the branch-merged-the-base shape only. Each is its own repo's card, at that shape.

### Security

- **card#8415 (DL-330)** — **the draft overlay stops resolving an author-supplied card id unscoped: the card#8375 board-scoped tenant check now runs on BOTH token-resolved writeback arms.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE DRAFT OVERLAY ACCEPTS.** `kanban_block_reason` (the opt-in `draft_overlay`) took its card id from the same author-controlled `card#`/DL token grammar as `kanban_move_card`, against the same kanban id space that is **GLOBAL across every board on the instance**, and resolved it with the same unscoped `GET /tasks/{id}.json` that DL-323 removed from the move handler — so the belongs-to-mapped-board compare's precondition was again a **successful cross-tenant read**, and on a 403 the compare was not reached at all. DL-323 named this arm as the same defect shape and deliberately left it open; that bound is now spent. The overlay now asks `GET /tasks/search.json?q=board_id=<mapped> id=<card>&limit=1` **first** and refuses an id that does not come back, **without ever issuing the unscoped read** — the same `MappedBoardGuard::refusesCardIdOutsideMappedBoard()` primitive, so the verdict-off-the-rows rule, both sides of the archive switch, the three refusal reasons and the transient/permanent split are inherited rather than re-implemented. **Newly refused:** a delivery whose card id does not resolve on the repo's mapped board (on a correctly-scoped install that set is **empty**); it now refuses under `card_id_outside_mapped_board`, `mapped_board_unreadable_to_this_token`, `board_scope_lookup_unfiltered` or `boardscope_*`, each **withholding the card id from the alert channel** (DL-314) and keeping it in the `Log::warning`. **Also narrowed, in the reporting direction only:** this arm's `getCard` 403 is now `getcard_403_token_scope` — the board-scoped check has already read the id back off the mapped board, so the foreign-card-id cause is excluded by a measurement. ⚠ **A CONSUMER MATCHING `getcard_403_foreign_card_id_or_token_scope` ON `kanban_block_reason` WILL STOP MATCHING** — the same break v0.80.0 shipped for `kanban_move_card`, now on the second and last arm that emitted it — and the two-cause slug is therefore emitted by **no shipped arm** (kept, and pinned by `RefusalContextTest`, as the honest answer for a future arm that establishes nothing before its read). **No accept path changes:** a card on the mapped board still gets its add-if-missing SET and clear-if-ours CLEAR exactly as before. **Cost:** one additional kanban read per overlay delivery that reaches a client. **No migration, no new config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change.** DL-323's two narrowed populations (a soft-deleted card on the mapped board; a writeback token that is super-admin but not a MEMBER of the mapped board) apply here identically. **The audit that came with it found no third arm of this shape** — over the six call sites of the client's only per-id read that names no board, the other four resolve their ids from board-scoped reads and already re-check the rows through the same guard (DL-298), or read back an id the caller itself just created.

### Docs

- **card#8378** — **`docs/kanban-integration-contract.md`'s `PATCH /api/v3/tasks/{id}.json` row was stale before this change and is corrected.** It said the bridge used that endpoint to *"Move a card"* with body *"`{workflow_stage_id}` **only**"* — untrue since DL-193's `block_reason` overlay and the correlation-ref payload delta, both of which PATCH the same endpoint. It now names every field write the bridge makes, the one client primitive that constructs them, and the two **opposite** field semantics on that endpoint (`payload` merges per key — kanban #2180; **`tags` is replaced wholesale**, which is what `board_correct_card`'s tag preservation exists for). §3's `Move = workflow_stage_id-only PATCH` invariant is about the MOVE and is unchanged — the two rows are now stated so they cannot read as contradicting.
- **card#8378** — **`docs/writeback.md`'s definition of what the write census counts as a write VERB was falsified by this change and is corrected.** It said *"every `KanbanClient` method whose body issues `->patch(` / `->post(` / `->delete(`"* — true only while every write verb spelled the verb itself, and no longer true the moment `moveCard` / `setBlockReason` / `stampCorrelationRefs` delegated to `patchCard`. It now states the TRANSITIVE rule the derivation actually applies (a method that reaches a mutating verb, followed through `$this-><method>(` to a fixed point) and points at the docblock that owns it. Reported by the review of a parallel branch reading the same sentence.

## [0.80.0] - 2026-09-01

### Added

- **card#8336 (DL-322)** — **`php artisan bridge:sign --scope=<scope>`: the deployment smoke test's signature, produced without the HMAC secret ever becoming a command-line argument.** Reads the raw body from **stdin** (or `--body-file`), resolves the per-`(provider, scope)` secret from its own file, and prints the complete `sha256=<hex>` header value — **stdout carries that and nothing else**, so `SIG=$(printf '%s' "$BODY" | php artisan bridge:sign --provider=github --scope="$SCOPE")` is safe to paste; every diagnostic goes to **stderr** and names the receiver status the same fault would produce there (`401 unknown_scope`, `500 secret_perms_insecure`, …) so a resolution fault here is not debugged at the receiver. It shares `WebhookSecretResolver` + `HmacSignature` with the middleware that verifies, so the three rules a hand-rolled signer gets wrong — the secret file's **trim**, the `sha256=` prefix, and the `%2F`-encoded filename for a scope containing `/` — cannot drift between producer and verifier. No config key, no migration, **no receiver accept/reject change**.

### Security

- **card#8336 (DL-322)** — **the runbook stopped telling operators to put a live HMAC webhook secret into `openssl`'s argv, where any local user reads it.** `CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md` § *Smoke-test the receiver with a signed delivery* said `SECRET=$(cat …)` then `openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$SECRET"`; the shell expands the value before `openssl` starts, and `/proc` on the reference deployment host has **no `hidepid`** (measured, not assumed), so the key that authenticates deliveries was readable from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` by every local account for the life of that process — routing around the `chmod 600` the receiver fail-closes on. The block now signs through `bridge:sign`. ⚠ **If you have ever run that smoke test with a corrected secret path, treat the scope's secret as disclosed and rotate it** — this change removes the way to leak it and makes no claim about whether it already was. This **discharges the one member DL-321 disclosed and left untouched**; the rule itself is not restated here — `docs/config-schema.md § Handling a secret VALUE` owns it and the runbook links there. The sibling sweep found no other argv member left in this repo (the provisioning scripts are clean), and the doc-fence lint DL-321 declined stays declined — the class control is card#8351's. → *Additive annotation, written after this release shipped (the sentences above are the released text and are not edited):* **the CI backstop landed after 0.80.0** — see **[Unreleased]** card#8351 / DL-324.
