- **card#8351 (DL-321)** — **three shipped runbooks told the reader to resolve a live secret onto a readable surface; the class is now stated once and the three sites follow it.** Docs-only: no code, no config key, no migration, no receiver accept/reject change, no token-scope change, and no exit code moves. **The live one was `docs/multi-host.md`**, whose channel-token step ended `echo "Save this token securely: $BRIDGE_CHANNEL_TOKEN"` — correct-looking guidance for a human at a terminal, and a live token written into the session transcript when the reader is an AI agent executing the runbook, which is the whole hazard. It now generates into a `0600` file under `umask 077`, exports the value for the rest of that shell only, and tells the operator to open the file themselves; the token is still obtainable, which is the constraint that ruled out simply deleting the `echo`. **Two more in the same file, both found by the sibling audit rather than reported:** the smoke-test `curl` passed the bearer as `-H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN"`, putting it in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` where every local account can read it for the life of the process — it now rides `curl --config -` on stdin (measured: the old form yields 1 match for the token in the running process's `cmdline`, the new form 0) — and the 401 row of the failure-mode table said *"Compare both values"*, an instruction whose only execution prints two secrets; it now compares `sha256sum` digests. **`docs/provider-adapters.md`** and **`docs/writeback.md`** each placed a secret as a command-line literal (`echo -n "your-hmac-secret" > …`, `install … <<<'<the-token>'`), which is not an argv leak — `echo` is a builtin and `install` reads stdin — but is written verbatim into the operator's shell history; both now take the value through `read -rs` and write it with `printf`. **The class, not the four lines, is what is recorded:** `docs/config-schema.md § Handling a secret VALUE` now owns the rule — stated over the SURFACE (stdout, argv, a log, shell history) rather than over an enumeration of instruments, because the next instrument that resolves a secret is never on the list — and the sites link to it instead of restating it. `CLAUDE_CONVENTIONS.md § Security / secrets conventions` carries the author-facing half. ⚠ **Nothing here is enforced by a check**, so a future runbook can still mint instance five; the harness-level controls that would close it (a PostToolUse redactor, a PreToolUse deny) change what the harness refuses and stay on card#8351 for an operator ruling. ⚠ **`CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md`'s GitHub smoke test passed the HMAC secret in `openssl dgst -hmac "$SECRET"` — the same argv shape, deliberately NOT touched by this change**: it was card#8336's, and **card#8336 lands in this same release** (see the entry above), so the member this one disclosed and left open is closed here too. → *Additive annotation, written after this release shipped (the sentences above are the released text and are not edited):* the *"Nothing here is enforced by a check"* clause describes the state **at 0.80.0** and no longer holds for **markdown shell fences** — **the CI backstop landed after 0.80.0** (see **[Unreleased]** card#8351 / DL-324). It still holds everywhere else the class can be minted (a `.php`, a `.sh`, a workflow `run:` block, a hook script, prose), and the harness-level controls remain open on card#8351.

### Changed

- **card#8305** — **`bridge:check` closes the last empty cell of the coord-card gate1/gate2 matrix, and the three copies of the derivation that kept minting it are hoisted behind one primitive.** ⚠ **This changes what the command reports as a problem** on an install that enables `coord-card-create` on a scope whose mapping never set `create_coord_cards`; **no exit code moves in either direction** (`warn` has never moved one), `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**, the registered check set stays **38**, and all 41 golden fixtures are byte-identical. **The leg.** `CoordinationClassifier::coordCardCreateFamily()` dispatches on the family and then returns null at its own mapping gate, so such an install delivers `issues.opened`/`reopened`, classifies nothing and cards nothing — with no error, no retry, and until now no line from the one command whose job is to say the writeback is wired. `coord-card-move` has had exactly this arm since DL-204 and `coord-card-relane` since card#6393, so the asymmetry was an omission and not a ruling; it is a `warn` for the same reason card#8292's mirror is one — a leg the operator explicitly turned on is DEAD, which is the condition this check's warning family is defined by. The line names both keys, `coord_card_stage_id` (a mapping that sets `create_coord_cards` without it fails the config closed at load, so a remediation naming only the first key sends the operator into a refused config), and the `docs/writeback.md` section that owns them. ⛔ **It is deliberately NOT a card#5698 disclosure when an agent goes unread**, inheriting the DL-204 arm's own no-disclosure ruling by name rather than re-arguing it: this leg's family term is a POSITIVE map read, so an unread agent cannot make it accuse anything — only leave it SILENT, which is the same silence it keeps for every install that does not enable the family. A disclosure here would print on every mapping that cards no coord issues — the majority — on any run with one unreadable agent config. **The consolidation, and why the two had to land together.** `CheckCommand::handle()` held the create / move / relane family→scope-map derivations as three byte-near-identical blocks differing only in a family string and a target map, and that third copy is what let the create family ship with only one of its two legs; they are now one `CheckContext::recordCoordCardFamilies()` call, with the subscription walk behind `CheckContext::githubScopes()` and the writeback-emitting map reusing it. Behaviour unchanged and measured as such: 41/41 golden fixtures byte-identical, plus a new `CheckContextTest` whose eight properties (per-family isolation, non-github exclusion, the DL-293 key form, accumulation across agents) were each watched RED against a mutated primitive — the create and relane halves of that derivation had no instrument at all before, the golden corpus enabling only `coord-card-move`. ⚠ **`docs/check-golden-coverage.{json,md}` went further out of date and said so in its own banner, with the new figures:** the live enumeration goes **59 → 49**, which against the 56 the artifact measured is **8 departures and 1 arrival**. The hoisted predicates leave that artifact's `handle()`-bounded region entirely, so the disclosed-gap rows they occupied were **UNMEASURED, not closed** — and **card#8304 regenerated the artifact in this same release**, which is what this sequencing existed to make payable once; the published figures are current with the 49 predicates as shipped.

- **card#8306 (DL-318)** — **a REVERT stops promoting the card it reverts.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK ACTS ON** (operator-approved). No migration, no config key, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change, and the `pr-title-lint` accept-set is byte-untouched. **The hazard is one card#8294 minted:** GitHub composes a revert PR by **quoting** the original's title and **wrapping** its branch, so once titles carry `(closes card#N)` every revert inherits a closing form — and a card token — **for work it UNDOES**. Merging one moved the reverted card to Shipped, so the board asserted the opposite of the truth. ⛔ **Both closure routes fired, and a title-only repair would have left half of it open:** measured end-to-end through the shipped classifier before any code changed, the lexical route closed on the quoted verb AND the structural route closed on the wrapped ref, because `CardTokenGrammar::parse('revert-611-card-8294-slug')` returns 8294 — the `card-<id>-slug` spelling `board-card-start` still mints. A nested `Revert "Revert "…""` moved it too. **The shapes are OBSERVED, not assumed:** no revert PR exists on any repo this shop owns (1,607 PRs scanned, zero) and GitHub's docs state neither format, so both were pulled through the REST API from public repositories where GitHub minted them (`laravel/framework#61330` → title `Revert "<the original's title>"` on `revert-61320-fix/contains-strict-comparison`; `#61262` → the branch-deleted `revert-61245`). **The fix** is a new `RevertGrammar` owning both artifacts of the one upstream act, consulted by the two authorities that already own the routes — `ClosureGrammar` subtracts the quoted span at its single choke point, `PrOutcome::mergeClosesCard()` asks `RevertGrammar::isRevert()` on **both** surfaces — so the event path and `bridge:reconcile` get the ruling from the same place and cannot disagree (the DL-305 §6 lockstep). ⛔ **The structural half must read the TITLE too, and a ref-only test was measured insufficient:** a HAND-MADE `git revert` wraps no ref, so under a ref-only gate `card-4811-widget`, `revert/card-4811-widget` and `revert-card-4811-widget` all still closed the card — silently, since a closing card never reaches the withheld-merge warning. `card-<id>-slug` is what `board-card-start` mints, so that was the common branch shape. The price is priced: a title merely QUOTING `revert "…"` loses its structural route, which appears in 0 of 1,566 merged PR titles (measured, with a planted control). ⛔ **CORRELATION IS UNTOUCHED**: a revert still stamps PR refs, and the `opened` outcome still fires — though the emitted `opened` move is then refused by the handler's no-regression guard whenever the card already sits at or past In-Review, which is the dominant case. Only the completion claim is refused. ⛔ **A revert of a revert does NOT close either** — GitHub does not escape the inner quotes so the depth is unparseable, and the first revert never moved the card back, so the re-apply has nothing to promote. ⭐ **To close a card ON a revert, put the closing form OUTSIDE the quoted title** (`Revert "…" — deliberate, this completes it (closes card#4811)`); no new marker was invented, and card#8294's `[no-close]` stays the CI-only suppression it was **[superseded by DL-327 — since card#8344 the writeback reads that marker too and withholds the merge move; it can only ever withhold one]**. ⚠ **Two operator-facing lines this change would have made FALSE are corrected in it** — the withheld-merge warning and the reconcile skip line both asserted *the ref does not name the card and the title carries no closing form*, untrue on both counts for a revert; every non-revert line is byte-identical. The `pr-title-lint` comment disclosing this hazard is discharged in place; its `revert-*` exemption stays, for its own reason (it governs what CI DEMANDS, never what the writeback READS). ⚠ **A divergence stays open and is filed, not repaired:** the lint decides *is this a revert* from the `revert-*` BRANCH and the runtime also reads the TITLE, so a hand-made `git revert` on an ordinary branch passes CI and is refused at runtime — under-promotion, loud and recoverable, and the same shape already recorded for the branch predicate.

- **card#8375 (DL-323)** — **the writeback resolves a card id THROUGH the mapped board instead of against it: a foreign card id is refused before it is ever read.** ⚠ **CHANGES WHAT THE WRITEBACK ACCEPTS.** `payload.card_id` reaches `kanban_move_card` as a literal parsed out of **author-controlled text** (`card#NNNN` in a PR title or head ref) against a kanban id space that is **GLOBAL across every board on the instance**, and it was resolved with an **unscoped** `GET /tasks/{id}.json` — so the belongs-to-mapped-board guard's precondition was *a successful cross-tenant read*, and on a 403 the guard was not reached at all. Measured live on a shared instance: a repo event on one install resolved to a card another install owns, and what stopped it was the API answering **403** — a property of the TOKEN, with nothing asserting its scope stays narrow and **no test going red if it widened**. The handler now establishes the id first, through a **board-scoped** `GET /tasks/search.json?q=board_id=<mapped> id=<card>` (both terms inside `q=`), and refuses an id that does not come back **without issuing the unscoped read at all**. ⛔ **The verdict is read off the ROWS, not off the call** — a row counts only if its own `id` names the card AND its own `board_id` is the mapped board — because that endpoint drops a filter it does not recognise and still answers 200. **On a correctly-scoped install the newly-refused set is EMPTY**; what changes is where the refusal happens and that it survives a widened token. **Both sides of the archive switch are asked** (DL-296 has no both-sides mode), so **an archived card on the mapped board still moves** — the archived probe runs only on a live miss, and the happy path costs **one** extra kanban read per delivery. **Three new refusal reasons, discriminated by a control rather than by inference:** `card_id_outside_mapped_board` (the board reads back, so the id is not this install's), `mapped_board_unreadable_to_this_token` (audit the writeback token's board membership first — an unreadable board and an empty one are one answer here), `board_scope_lookup_unfiltered` (the lookup answered somebody else's row: a broken read, never a tenant verdict), plus `boardscope_403_token_scope` / `_404_no_such_card` / `_4xx` when the scoped lookup is itself refused. All four withhold the card id from the alert channel (DL-314) and keep it in the log. ⚠ **A CONSUMER MATCHING `getcard_403_foreign_card_id_or_token_scope` ON `kanban_move_card` WILL STOP MATCHING:** that arm now emits **`getcard_403_token_scope`**, because the scoped check has already read the id back off the mapped board and the foreign-id cause is excluded by measurement. `kanban_block_reason`, which makes no such check, still emits the two-cause slug — **that arm is the same defect shape and is deliberately NOT fixed here**, disclosed in the handler, in `RefusalContext::readReason()` and in `docs/writeback.md`. ⚠ **Two smaller populations narrow with it, disclosed rather than chased:** a **soft-deleted** card on the mapped board (the unscoped read is routed `withTrashed()`, the search is not) and a writeback token whose user is a **super-admin but not a member** of the mapped board (kanban's search scopes to explicit board membership, never to super-admin mode) — both now refuse, and the second is already reported by `bridge:check`'s visibility probe at preflight. No migration, no config key, no `.env` change, no token-scope change.

### Docs

- **card#8304** — **`docs/check-golden-coverage.{json,md}` regenerated: the published measurement again describes the `handle()` that actually ships.** Docs-only — no `app/` change, no config key, no check registered, no exit code moved. The artifact had gone stale by card#8305's consolidation and said so in its own `SUBJECT-MOVED` banner; `php bin/check-golden-mutate.php` was re-run against `96391bc` and the banner is dropped, which is what it said an agreeing regeneration would do. **49 predicates — 44 observed, 5 UNOBSERVED**, and the arithmetic the banner predicted held exactly: 56 − 8 departures + 1 arrival = 49 (the departures are 3× `foreach $cfg->subscriptions`, 3× `if $sub->provider === 'github'` and the `coord-card-move` / `coord-card-relane` `in_array` tests; the arrival is `foreach CheckContext::githubScopes($cfg)`). **No surviving predicate changed verdict** — the run's own STOP condition, checked as a multiset on `(kind, source, status)` because keying on `(kind, source)` alone collapses the duplicate subscription walks and under-reports 8 departures as 2. `CheckCommand.php` was verified byte-identical (md5 `e66e8e4e…`) after the in-place mutation run. ⚠ **The disclosed-gap count falls 7 → 5 by REMOVAL, not by CLOSURE** — nothing was measured as newly covered; the two that vanished are the predicate pair card#8305 consolidated out of existence. The remaining five are the abort walk, the spellings walk and `$configs !== []`.

- **card#8286 (DL-319), card#8294** — **decision-log and CI-comment follow-ups to work that shipped in v0.79.0.** No behaviour change of any kind. `CLAUDE_DECISIONS.md` gains **DL-319** (the release-promote tag gate: `release-promote-cards.yml` and `auto-tag-version.yml` both fire on `push: main` with no `needs:` between them, so they race, and the promote run is now gated behind the tag it is about to assert) and an **additive annotation on DL-023** recording that its phrase *"isolated from auto-tag"* is falsified as a safety property — the two workflows are independent, and independence is not isolation. `pr-title-lint`'s tie claim is scoped to the closure predicate it actually measures, with the test widened to hold it. The solo-orientation block in `CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md` is re-stamped to coord v0.48.0.

## [0.79.0] - 2026-08-31

### Added

- **card#7992 (DL-317)** — **`docs/check-golden-coverage.md` gains a second currency guard, over WHICH predicates it measured, and now states in its own header what is guarded and what is not.** ⛔ **Tests, docs and the generator's template only — no `app/` change, no migration, no config key, no check registered, no severity or exit-code move, nothing the receiver accepts or rejects.** **The gap.** `CheckGoldenCoverageCurrencyTest` pins the header's predicate COUNT against the live enumerator and reds in both directions — correct, and relied on — but a count is a DENOMINATOR: it cannot see a predicate set that changed IDENTITY while keeping its SIZE. ⚑ **Not hypothetical:** between two regenerations of this artifact one predicate DEPARTED while two arrived, so until the next run the published disclosed-gap table named a condition (`$configs !== [] && $ctx->configDir !== null`) that existed nowhere in the source — and that column is exactly what someone refactoring greps for, so a rotted row either finds nothing and reads as the reader's own mistake, or matches a different predicate that was never measured. The count moved by one that time and caught it by luck; one departure against one arrival is the same defect with the count standing still. **`CheckGoldenCoverageSubjectTest`** compares the artifact's measured `(kind, source)` MULTISET against the same enumerator the generator drives. ⭐ **A SEPARATE term with its own banner, not a widened one** — the two must be independently satisfiable, because the case it exists for is precisely the one where the count agrees and the currency guard therefore requires ITS banner ABSENT; one shared string would deadlock the pair. `(kind, source)` and not the predicate id, because ids embed a line offset and would red on pure line drift; a MULTISET and not a set, because several predicates share one condition text verbatim and deduplicating would hide a departure behind its own twin. ⛔ **A cheap guard on the VERDICTS was considered and DECLINED, on the record.** The candidate was a fingerprint over the measurement's inputs; the input closure of a verdict is the whole `bridge:check` plane plus its config defaults and the golden corpus, so a fingerprint over any nameable SUBSET carries false negatives it cannot state — the same stated-scope-exceeds-predicate defect one level down — while a fingerprint over the whole closure reds on essentially every check-plane change and, with a ~57-minute run as the only remedy, would leave the banner permanently up. ⚑ **What ships instead is disclosure:** the artifact's header now says the two guards cover its SUBJECT, that nothing re-derives `observed` / `observed-via-abort` / `UNOBSERVED`, and that a regenerated golden capture, a changed check implementation or a changed config default can move a verdict without any guard going red — so a green suite is evidence the file describes THIS `handle()`, and no evidence about how current the three columns are. The sentence is added to the generator's template and the committed artifact identically, so a regeneration reproduces it byte-for-byte.

### Changed

- **card#8142** — **`docs/check-golden-coverage.{json,md}` regenerated against current `dev`, so the published measurement is this branch's rather than the one taken before card#7992's generator changes landed.** ⛔ **Generated artifacts only — no `app/` change, no `bin/` change, no test change; nothing the receiver accepts or rejects, and no severity or exit-code move.** **Every verdict held.** All 56 predicates keep their id, kind, line, condition text, `status` and `failing` set — 49 `observed`, 0 `observed-via-abort`, 7 `UNOBSERVED`, and the seven disclosed gaps are the same seven. The run was diffed field-by-field against the committed artifact by an instrument that was itself fire-tested in three directions first (a poisoned verdict, a poisoned key set, and a no-op) and that carries an in-run control poisoning row 0; it reports **zero** verdict moves. ⚑ **The only content delta is the `errors` key**, now emitted as `0` on all 56 records — the visible product of card#8019's move to reading verdicts from phpunit's own `--log-junit` output, which the previous artifact predates. The `.md` moves by one line, the generator-emitted wall-clock (57 → 60 minutes). ⭐ **Both currency guards were seen to FAIL before this was trusted** — `CheckGoldenCoverageCurrencyTest` reds on a poisoned header count, `CheckGoldenCoverageSubjectTest` reds on a dropped predicate record — so their green here is evidence the artifact describes THIS `handle()`, not a vacuous pass. Per the file's own header the verdicts remain unguarded by construction; this entry is the record of when they were last re-derived.
- **card#8276** — **the two `PupFuzz/agent-board-toolkit` composite-action pins move 0.29.0 → 0.31.0** (`release-artifacts` in `release-artifacts-gate.yml`, `promote` in `release-promote-cards.yml`; both to `d792a821537a3fbdd05a54357c2669626b1d627a`, the v0.31.0 release commit, with the load-bearing `# vX.Y.Z` comment moved in the same edit). ⛔ **CI-only — nothing about an installed bridge changes:** no `app/` change, no migration, no config key, no route, nothing the receiver accepts or rejects, and no operator-facing surface moves. **Neither action's INTERFACE moved between the two pins** — `release-artifacts/action.yml` still declares `base-sha` / `head-sha` / `config` and `promote/action.yml` still declares `writeback-token` / `expected-host` / `api-base` / `dls` / `shipped-stage-ids` / `dry-run`, with identical `required:` and `default:` on every one, and neither declares an output; both call sites are unchanged apart from the SHA. **Two behaviour changes ride along and both are no-ops for this repo, checked rather than assumed.** (1) `release-artifacts-check` (toolkit card#6488 / card#7599) stops narrowing the extracted version with a second hardcoded regex and, on an unchanged first match, now compares the two ends' SETS of `version_regex` matches so a real release PR is not read as “version unchanged”. This repo's `VERSION` is a single line carrying exactly one `[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+` match, so the new branch resolves to the same verdict the old code gave and the multi-match refusal is unreachable here. (2) `promote/action.yml` (toolkit card#7494) stops rewriting `.promote.api_base` into the checked-out `.release-pr.json` with `jq` and passes `api-base` to the script as `$KANBAN_API_BASE`, which `bin/promote-released-cards` reads in preference to the committed value — `release-promote-cards.yml` already supplies it out-of-band from `vars.KANBAN_API_BASE` behind a preflight that refuses a blank, so the resolved host is the same and the workspace is no longer mutated mid-job. **Why a hand bump at all:** dependabot's `github-actions` ecosystem does track this `owner/repo/subdir@sha` shape and has opened a bump PR for both actions on the weekly cadence since adoption (most recently #583 / #584, 0.28.0 → 0.29.0, on 2026-08-24). v0.30.1 and v0.31.0 were tagged after that run and the next scheduled one had not yet fallen, so the staleness is a between-runs lag rather than a gap in coverage — stated in `release-artifacts-gate.yml` beside the pin so a future reader does not have to re-derive it. ⚠ One measured exception, reported rather than fixed here because nothing in this repo can act on it: **v0.30.0 was tagged 2026-08-24T00:45Z, about nine hours BEFORE that day's run, and the run still targeted 0.29.0** — every other run since adoption picked the newest tag then existing, so the mechanism is a lag of unestablished cause, not a blockage.

- **card#8292** — **`bridge:check` gains its first leg for the coord-card CREATE family: a mapping that sets `create_coord_cards` while no agent enables `coord-card-create` is now WARNED as the inert leg it is.** ⚠ **This changes what the command reports as a problem** on an install carrying that shape; **no exit code moves in either direction** (`warn` has never moved one), `--format=json` `schema` stays 1, no check is registered (one more leg on the existing `writeback.mapping_config`), and no migration, config key or receiver accept/reject behaviour changes. **The gap.** The DL-204 pair covered `move_coord_cards` in both directions and card#8290 covered the lane model, but there was **no leg at all** for the create family — and **no `coordCardCreateScopes` map in `CheckContext` to build one from**, so the instrument did not exist rather than the condition being unwritten. `CoordinationClassifier` dispatches on the family **before** it reads the mapping, so an install with gate 2 on and gate 1 off classifies nothing and creates nothing: `issues.opened`/`reopened` arrive, no card is made, and there is no error, no retry and no signal from the one command whose job is to say the writeback is wired. ⭐ **A `warn`, where its two immediate neighbours are `ok`, and the difference is the whole ruling** — card#8290's lane-model line and the card#7348 / DL-305 mention-vs-closure line speak about installs that are entirely CORRECT and every leg they configured fires; here a leg the operator explicitly turned on is DEAD, which is exactly the condition this check's warning family is defined by (*"every condition there makes some writeback leg silently INERT"*), so the warn accuses nothing correct. Severity follows the deadness of the leg, never the interestingness of the fact. **Silent on a fully configured install** (family enabled), pinned by its own control, and **degrades to `unvalidated` with the shared gap clause and no remediation** when an agent config this run could not read might be the one enabling the family (card#5698) — an unread agent is never warned about. The remediation names both keys and the `docs/writeback.md` section that owns them, asserted by content. **One golden fixture moved** (`writeback-half-configured-triggers` gains the line; its exit code is unchanged at 0). ⚠ **`docs/check-golden-coverage.{json,md}` is now STALE and says so in two banners**: the new scope map is derived in `CheckCommand::handle()`'s per-agent loop, so the measured predicate set gained three members (56 → 59, no departures); the ~60-minute mutation regeneration is deferred, not the false number. **⛔ Sibling gap reported and NOT built:** the opposite direction (an agent enabling `coord-card-create` on a scope whose mapping does not set `create_coord_cards`) has no leg either, though the `coord-card-move` family has exactly that arm — deliberately kept out of this change.
- **card#8290** — **`bridge:check` now names the coord-card race a lane model does NOT close, on the installs that have only half of it.** A mapping that sets `coord_card_lane_stage_ids` while no agent enables the `coord-card-relane` family draws one green setup-time line: the lane a coord card sits in is written when the card is **created** and on a revive, **never when a `stage:*` label arrives afterwards** — so a `[TASK]` labelled after its card exists keeps the lane it was born in (`later` for an issue opened with no lane label), and on an install whose consumer writes a card's lane back onto the issue, the label the operator just set is converged **back** to that lane. ⛔ **`ok`, not a warning — no exit code moves in either direction, and no config is accused.** The install is correct and leaving the family off stays a valid choice (it is the only family that reacts to a label edit, and `issues.labeled` is a high-volume action); what an operator cannot learn from their own `writeback.json` is that the family exists at all, which is the card#7348 / DL-305 shape and gets that shape's severity. **A fully adopted install — both keys set, family enabled — stays silent**, which is the measurement the card#6393 decline of a WARN here turned on, pinned by its own test. The line additionally names `move_coord_cards` where the mapping does not set it (a create-only lane model is valid since DL-294, and the family needs the move flag), and degrades to an `unvalidated` disclosure when an agent config this run could not read might be the one enabling the family (card#5698). Filed after a peer operator spent a full investigation cycle diagnosing this race as a missing mechanism — for a family this bridge had already shipped and released. **One leg on the existing `writeback.mapping_config` check**: no new check, no registry move, the check count is unchanged and `--format=json` `schema` stays 1.

- **card#8286** — **the release-promote job REFUSES to stamp cards "Released to main" until the release's tag actually exists.** ⚠ **CI-accepts change, and it is on the merge-to-`main` push only — nothing about an installed bridge, no `app/` change, no migration, no config key, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change.** `release-promote-cards.yml` and `auto-tag-version.yml` share the `push: main` trigger with **no `needs:` between them**, so they are two independent runs and promote wins the race on **every** release: the board was stamped against a tag that did not exist yet, and where the tag never arrived the assertion was simply false with nothing to correct it. **Measured in `agent-board-toolkit` on its v0.28.0 merge:** auto-tag's push took a **403**, promote reported "2 moved" and SUCCESS three seconds later, and that board asserted a shipped release for **17m27s** until a human re-ran the job. The fix adopts the toolkit's third composite action, `release-tag-check` (SHA-pinned `d792a821537a3fbdd05a54357c2669626b1d627a`  # v0.31.0, per rule 8), as a step **before** the promote step. **It WAITS rather than refusing on sight** — because the two runs start together the tag is legitimately absent for the first seconds of every release, so an immediate hard-refuse would red them all; the bound and what happens at it belong to the tool's own header and are deliberately not restated in this repo. **A non-release push to `main` is not gated**: the check delegates "is this range a release" to `release-artifacts-check --classify-only`, which decides it from `.release-pr.json`'s `version_file`/`version_regex` at both ends of the range, so only a push that moves `VERSION` waits for anything. `workflow_dispatch` runs skip the gate entirely (no before-ref to classify). ⛔ **Deliberately NOT fixed by reordering the workflows or adding a `sleep`** — a delay shrinks the window and makes the failure rarer, and a rare false "Released to main" is harder to catch than a common one. **Permissions are unchanged** (`contents: read` is sufficient — the gate polls refs with `git ls-remote` and writes nothing), and the `v*` tag ruleset blocks only tag **update** and **deletion**, so neither tag creation nor this gate's read path is affected. ⚑ **What a red gate means: check whether the tag exists — not that a board write failed. The refusal is loud in Actions and silent on the board; the cards simply stay where they were.** New `ReleaseTagGateTest` asserts what the workflow cannot assert about itself, on a `push: main` path no PR can rehearse: the gate exists, runs **before** promote, is guarded to the `push` event, carries a `timeout-minutes` backstop, the checkout it classifies from is unshallow, and — the declare/check pair this adoption MINTS — **the tag the gate waits for is the tag the tagger creates**. `tag_format` tells the gate what to expect and changes nothing about what `auto-tag-version.yml` creates, so adding the key without moving the tagger would make the gate wait for a tag nobody mints and refuse **every** release at its bound; both sides are derived from the artifacts, so the leg reds whichever moves alone. Mutation-proven **9/9** (step deleted, step moved after promote, `if:` dropped, backstop dropped, `fetch-depth` shallowed, `tag_format` moved alone, tagger moved alone, tagger stops assigning `TAG=` at all) with a control that stays green when both tag-name sides move together. `VERSIONING.md` step 14 records the new post-merge outcome.
- **card#8289** — **the writeback's pinned-card opt-out is consulted on EVERY move outcome, not only `started`.** ⚠ **A behaviour change in the REFUSING direction, and one that REVERSES a recorded ruling:** DL-178 scoped the pin to `started` on purpose (*"pinning is about not un-parking a card, not about freezing its PR-driven lifecycle"*). **What made that untenable is that two of the pin's three consumers had already widened it while citing DL-178** — `bridge:reconcile` (DL-183) skips a pinned card unconditionally, on all outcomes, and DL-207's release-promote sweep does the same — so the live system had **the reconciler skipping what the event path moved**, and the backstop could not repair the event path's move. **Measured against `origin/dev` @ `1721f36` with a control:** a card carrying `block_reason` *and* a `no-automove` tag sent **no PATCH** on `started` (the instrument works) and `PATCH {"workflow_stage_id":52}` on `merged` — the pin was bypassed on exactly the terminal, hard-to-reverse move. **Now:** a non-empty `block_reason` **or** a `no-automove` tag refuses `started`, `opened`, `merged`, `merged_to_main`, `closed_unmerged` and the opt-in `reopened` alike, loudly (`Log::warning` + the `alert_channel` signal `pinned_no_automove`, whose message names the outcome and whose `(repo, outcome, reason)` dedup makes each outcome signal once). `closed_unmerged` is included deliberately — it is the lone legitimately-backward outcome, and a hold holds in both directions. **The two existing overrides are untouched:** a `started` from an `unpark_from_stages` stage (DL-194) and a `reopened` revival from the mapped `closed_unmerged` stage (DL-195, whose alternative (b) *"respect the pin"* the operator explicitly rejected) still move a pinned card and still emit their compensating alert. ⭐ **The pin governs the card's STAGE, not its correlation refs** — a refused move still stamps `dl_number`/`pr_number`/`pr_url` add-if-missing. Dropping the stamp too would put the held card **outside `bridge:reconcile`'s population** (it reconciles only a card carrying a resolvable repo+PR), and since GitHub delivers each PR event exactly once the backstop could then never complete the move after the human lifted the pin — the hold would be a severance rather than a pause. ⚠ **Operator-visible:** if you pin cards with `block_reason` for reasons unrelated to holding their position, their PR-driven stage moves now stop until the pin clears; `bridge:reconcile --fix` moves them once it does. **No migration, no config key, no token-scope change, nothing new the receiver accepts or rejects.** This also makes DL-308's named card-side follow-up (*"a hold/pin marker the writeback refuses to move past"*) mostly a predicate move rather than a new mechanism — the marker shipped in 2026-07 and simply never fired on a merge.

- **card#8294** — **CI now reds a PR that names a card and claims closure on neither route, and the convention that depends on it is written down.** ⚠ **CI-accepts change — it changes what `pr-title-lint` refuses. Nothing about an installed bridge moves:** no `app/` behaviour change, no migration, no config key, no route, no token-scope change, nothing the receiver or the writeback accepts or rejects. **The defect it closes was silent and total.** Since DL-305/DL-308 a merge promotes a card only on **closure evidence** — the head ref names the card (structural) or the title carries a closing form naming it (lexical). Correlation never implied either, and nothing checked for it, so when this seat's branch convention moved from `card-<id>-slug` to `<type>/<id>-slug` on 2026-08-29 the structural route stopped firing on every PR at once while the titles carried a bare `(card#<id>)` — a correlation, never a closure (DL-305). **8 consecutive merges satisfied neither route**: each logged `mention-vs-closure, DL-305/DL-308`, moved nothing, and left its card in In Review with the PR stamp already on it; all 8 were corrected by hand. A **third** spelling (`card-sign-kbcard` — the `card` token with no digits) shows the same refusal predates the flip. ⭐ **The convention was not wrong; the fact that nothing DECLARED it and nothing CHECKED it is the root cause, and that is what this fixes.** `CLAUDE_CONVENTIONS.md` gains a **PR titles** section stating `<type>(<scope>): <subject> (closes card#<id>)` and, explicitly, that card promotion depends on it — pointing at `docs/writeback.md` § *Mention vs closure* for the rule rather than restating it — and its **Branch naming** section stops documenting a `feature/<short-slug>` shape this repo abandoned. **The check** is a third step in `.github/workflows/pr-title-lint.yml`: a PR whose TITLE correlates a card must close it on one of the two routes or red. ⛔ **A PR that correlates NO card still passes untouched** (docs chores, dependabot, every tokenless title), and a PR citing a card it **deliberately does not finish** — the context-PR case, which must stay possible or the gate becomes an over-promotion machine — declares that with the literal **`[no-close]`** in the title; that marker suppresses the CHECK only, nothing at runtime reads it **[superseded by DL-327 — since card#8344 the writeback reads the same marker and withholds the merge move; it can only ever withhold one]**. **Measured over this repo's last 120 merged PRs: 49 pass, 24 red, 12 carry no card token, 35 exempt** — the red set is the defect population and its predecessors, so the gate discriminates rather than decorating. **Neither bash grammar is kept in lockstep by discipline:** `PrTitleLintTest` extracts the step's `closure=` and `token=` regexes and compares their ANSWER SETS against `ClosureGrammar` and `CardTokenGrammar`, over each authority's own vectors and over the whole printable-ASCII separator domain; it compares the step's card-token regex against the warn step's `good=` so this workflow cannot hold two accept-sets that disagree; it requires the quoted forms in the failure message to be forms the grammar accepts AND requires the message to name **every verb stem** the grammar accepts, derived from `ClosureGrammar::accepted()`; and the load-bearing leg compares the STEP's verdict to the BRIDGE's own CLOSURE predicate (`ClosureGrammar::closesCard()` OR `PrOutcome::mergeClosesCard()`) row by row. ⚠ **The tie is to that predicate, not to the writeback as a whole, and the difference is stated rather than left to be inferred:** a merge is also refused for reasons no PR title can express — an unmapped repo, a card the token cannot read, the mapped-board guard, a near-miss hijack, and (since card#8289, in this same `[Unreleased]`) a PINNED card on every outcome. So a green check here means *this PR claims closure*, never *this card will move*; the step's own success lines say `closes it structurally` / `carries a closing form`, and deliberately promise no move. **Mutation-proven 11/11** — a verb dropped from the closure regex, the verb/token adjacency loosened, the enumerated separator replaced by `[[:space:]]`, a verb dropped from the message, the token regex widened by one separator, the opt-out short-circuit deleted, the exemption `case` deleted, the no-card skip arm deleted, the release-base name changed, the base-ref conjunct dropped, and **each** of the two card-id comparisons replaced by an unconditional true — with a control that stays green unmutated. **Two of the eleven survived the first corpus**, which is why it now carries a title closing a *different* card than it correlates and a branch naming a *different* card than the title. ⛔ **The separator class is ENUMERATED, not `[[:space:]]`, and that is DL-272's ruling re-derived on a second bracket expression rather than assumed by analogy.** grep resolves `[[:space:]]` from LOCALE DATA: measured with the runner's own tool (GNU grep 3.11 — *not* this shell's Unicode-aware shim, and the instrument was the finding), under `C.UTF-8` (the Actions default) and `en_US.UTF-8` it admits **U+1680, U+2000, U+205F and U+3000**, none of which `ClosureGrammar` accepts, while agreeing under plain `C`. A title separated by one of those four would have PASSED the gate and been REFUSED by the writeback — the false green this step exists to delete. The price is pinned by name: `\s` also admits TAB/VT/FF/CR and the enumeration does not, so those red here and close at runtime — the false-RED direction, and only TAB can reach a one-line title at all. **The step reads the BASE ref as well as the title and branch**, because `PrOutcome::mergeClosesCard()` requires an INTEGRATION merge: without it the structural arm would mirror the predicate minus a conjunct and call the difference an assumption. ⚑ **Two further stale claims corrected where the sibling audit found them:** `PrOutcomeTest`'s *"the shape 92% of this shop's recent merged PRs carry"*, and **DL-308**, which reads *"on the live convention this takes the gate from 0% to 93.2%"* and *"the current one is `card-<id>-slug`"* — annotated ADDITIVELY per the log's own convention (the entry stays frozen), with the re-measurement and with the note that this change takes **DL-308's own first Alternative**, listed there as *"the honest option and the named fallback"*, as an ADDITION rather than a reversal: both routes still close and the structural term is untouched. ⚑ **Two stale claims corrected where they stood:** `PrOutcome::mergeClosesCard()`'s docblock and `docs/writeback.md` both priced the strict branch-token reading at *"about 1 merged PR in 59"*, a figure measured under the `card-<id>-slug` convention and false the day it flipped — both now state what the figure measured, that it went to 8 in 8, and that the predicate itself did not move. ⚠ **Two properties disclosed rather than fixed, both their own decisions:** the leg covers the **card** route only and stays silent on a title naming a `DL-<n>` that resolves to cards (measured: 2 of 120 titles carry a DL and no card token, both already closed structurally, while the shape a DL arm would have to red — the DL cited as provenance beside the work's own card — occurs 4 times, so a DL arm would trade a silent miss for a false red on a population of zero); and GitHub mints a **revert** PR's title by quoting the original's, so a revert of a closing PR arrives carrying that closing form and the RUNTIME predicate reads it the same way — the step exempts `revert-*` so CI does not demand more of them, which does not touch what the writeback does with the one GitHub writes by itself.

### Fixed

- **card#8019** — **`bin/check-golden-mutate.php` reads every verdict from phpunit's own `--log-junit` log instead of from the suite's stdout, and refuses an iteration that produced no readable log.** Developer-facing only: no `app/` change, no migration, no config key, no receiver accept/reject change, nothing about an installed bridge moves, and **neither coverage artifact is regenerated by this card** — a full run was made as PARITY EVIDENCE and the committed verdicts are unchanged. ⭐ **The defect is that the DEFAULT path was the broken one.** Every verdict was `json_decode()`d off the golden suite's stdout, and that JSON exists only while an installed result printer chooses to emit it — so the verdict depended on a property of the SHELL rather than of the mutant. Running the repo's own documented regeneration command in an ordinary operator terminal decoded nothing: **every red predicate scored `observed-via-abort` naming no fixture and every green one scored UNOBSERVED — two distinct statuses, so the `DEGENERATE RESULT SET` arm never fired and both artifacts were written at exit 0.** Over the corpus that is 49 rows of pure noise published as a measurement, and the artifact is the evidence base `docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md` cites. **The JUnit log is phpunit's own artifact, so it cannot drift with whichever result printer the tree happens to have installed** — the rule is not invented here, `tests/Support/NestedSuite.php` states it verbatim and is this repo's other caller that follows it; the fix removes the dependency at the root rather than detecting it. ⭐ **A run that wrote no readable log is now REFUSED, never scored** — the two verdicts an unmeasured iteration falls into are both false, and the silent one is `UNOBSERVED`, which renders as a *disclosed gap*, the strongest claim that artifact makes about a predicate, off evidence nobody read. The log is written outside the repo and cleared before every run, so a stale one cannot be read as the next predicate's verdict. **Two further wins the log carries over the report it replaces:** an ESCAPED abort — the mutant throwing past every fail-soft envelope, which phpunit records as an ERROR — now reaches the refusal with the errored testcase and its exception message NAMED, where the old report carried nothing to name; and `<error>` is a different element from `<failure>`, which is the mechanism (rather than a printer's key-emission rule) behind the empty `failing` list that refusal exists for. ⚠ **This supersedes one half of the card#7994 entry below:** that card's baseline control had two legs, and its *"the report is decodable"* leg screened for a dependency this card removes. **The leg it is replaced by is not the same leg** — it asks whether the suite wrote a readable log at all, which is not an environment property and has no `PAO_FORCE` remedy; what survives is cause SEPARATION, because every way phpunit fails to finish also exits non-zero, so without it the `red` term would report a healthy corpus as a broken one. **The other leg — the golden suite is green before any mutation — is untouched.** The script header's paragraph declaring the agent-shell dependency, the refusal banner's explanation and its `PAO_FORCE=1` remedy are all retired with it. **Measured, same tree, same predicate, same stripped shell** (`env -u CLAUDECODE -u AI_AGENT -u CURSOR_AGENT -u CLAUDE_CODE`): the pre-fix generator refuses at the baseline control, this one scores `if-L386` **`observed`** — the verdict the committed artifact holds. `CheckGoldenMutateVerdictIntegrityTest` grows to nine scenarios against the synthetic `--repo`, including one where the two candidate sources are made to DISAGREE — a lying stdout report naming a fixture the log does not — so the test discriminates rather than merely passing; the stub suite prints no JSON on stdout in any scenario, and refuses an invocation carrying no `--log-junit` outright. Each guard term was watched RED before it was trusted, by single-term mutation of the shipped script.

- **card#7994** — **`bin/check-golden-mutate.php` gains a BASELINE CONTROL, and stops publishing an `observed-via-abort` verdict that no fixture backs.** Developer-facing only: no `app/` change, no migration, no config key, no receiver accept/reject change, nothing about an installed bridge moves, and **neither coverage artifact is regenerated by this card**. ⭐ **The larger half is the control, and the defect it closes is that this generator only works in a shell the test runner treats as an AI agent's — a dependency neither its own header nor any doc had ever stated.** Every verdict it scores is read out of a JSON report on the golden suite's stdout, and `vendor/bin/phpunit` emits that report only under `laravel/pao`, which activates when `laravel/agent-detector` finds an agent environment variable (`CLAUDECODE`, `AI_AGENT`, `CURSOR_AGENT`, …) or when `PAO_FORCE` is set. **In an ordinary operator shell phpunit prints its classic text output** — measured: `PHPUnit 13.2.6 by Sebastian Bergmann and contributors.` — `json_decode` returns null, and every iteration falls to the *"could not even produce its report"* arm. ⛔ **Every red predicate then scores `observed-via-abort` naming no fixture and every green one `UNOBSERVED` — two distinct statuses, so the existing `DEGENERATE RESULT SET` arm does NOT fire and a full run writes both artifacts at exit 0.** Over the current corpus (49 `observed` + 7 `UNOBSERVED`) that is 49 abort rows of pure noise, published as a measurement. **One suite run against the UNMUTATED tree, before the loop, now refuses the whole run unless that run is green AND emitted a decodable report** — roughly 70 seconds bought against the ~57 minutes it guards — and it **names which cause it hit** instead of blaming the golden harness for both: *no decodable report* carries the `laravel/pao` explanation and the `PAO_FORCE=1` lever **[Precision correction (see card#8019, above): that leg does not ship in this form. The verdict source moved off the suite's stdout in the same unreleased window, so the leg asks whether the suite wrote a readable `--log-junit` log, and the `laravel/pao` explanation and the `PAO_FORCE=1` remedy are retired with the dependency they described. The second leg below is untouched.]**; *already red* names the failing fixtures and says every verdict the run would produce is contaminated (`red` is `exit != 0`, so under a red baseline `UNOBSERVED` is unreachable and every predicate scores off a failure that predates its mutation). It runs under `--only`/`--limit` too — a narrowed run prints verdicts a human reads, and they are exactly as false — and it `exit()`s rather than throwing, because it sits before the `try` with nothing yet written to the mutation target and so has no `finally` restore to skip. ⚠ **A claim the first cut of this work asserted in three places and that is FALSE, corrected here:** an `observed-via-abort` does **not** carry a non-empty failing list *by construction*. An abort a fail-soft envelope **RENDERED** does — the command still prints, the golden capture still differs, and the failure message carries both the `fixture '<name>'` the scrape reads and a fatal-error token. An abort that **ESCAPES `handle()`** does not: `handle()` has no top-level try/catch, `GoldenCapture::capture()` calls `Artisan::call` with no catch, and `Illuminate\Console\Application::call()` runs with `setCatchExceptions(false)`, so the throwable propagates out of the test and phpunit records an **ERROR**, not a failure — and `laravel/pao` writes the `failures` key only when something FAILED, so an all-errors report carries no `failures` at all and the list is necessarily empty. **That is a REAL abort the fixture set genuinely provoked**, exactly what the script's header says the status exists for. ⛔ **It is still refused — as a deliberate RULING, not because the case cannot arise.** The row it would render asserts *reached, and the guard is load-bearing*, and with no fixture named nothing the fixture set produced backs either half; an abort with zero fixture-level evidence gives the artifact nothing to publish, so the chosen failure mode is to refuse loudly and let the operator read the error and decide by hand. (Not reachable on the tree last measured, which is all that can be said: the committed artifact holds no abort record of EITHER shape, so no predicate in it aborts at all — a fact about that corpus and that `handle()`, not a property of the code.) The refusal's message says as much — with a green, decodable baseline behind it the environment is ruled out, so it **enumerates** the remaining candidates rather than asserting one, printing that iteration's own `decodable` and `errors` facts beside them. ⛔ **The whole run is refused, at the first such predicate, not the one row** — the generated header makes a whole-population claim (*"over the N branch predicates in `handle()`"*), so one unmeasured predicate falsifies the document, and a rendered row is indistinguishable from the real ones. It **throws** rather than `exit()`s so the `finally` restore still runs and no mutant is left on disk, and it reuses the existing `ABORTED` report rather than adding a second failure shape. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT restricted by predicate kind** — now witnessed rather than merely recorded: the test's synthetic source grew a `foreach` so the ruling is actually exercised. ⚑ **A stated BOUND, not a fix — the MIXED shape is not caught:** `errors` above zero *with* a non-empty failing list (some fixtures errored, others reached a golden diff) renders as an ordinary `observed-via-abort` whose evidence is partial, and the artifact said nothing about that. Each result record now carries the run's **`errors` count**, which makes the gap auditable after the fact instead of invisible; the general *"was this iteration real"* term stays open. **The verdict this card started from is cited by its CONDITION — the `CheckSlot::ProbeTools` `emitReport` arm — and never by its row id**, which is line-anchored and **has already rotted**: that id now belongs to the same predicate scored `observed` over 32 fixtures, so a reader grepping it finds a healthy row. It came from run 1 of card#7835's regeneration (committed on that card's branch and superseded there before merge); what did reach `dev` is `docs/CHECK-REGISTRY-PLAN.md`'s record of the two runs disagreeing, where the row was reasoned about as a result before an adversarial review caught it. ⛔ **Neither guard DIAGNOSES that run:** it scored 48 `observed` rows carrying real fixture lists, so its report emitter was working and a whole-run environment failure is ruled out by measurement — the baseline control closes a different and larger shape, and run 1's single iteration is the residue the in-loop refusal now refuses rather than renders. `CheckGoldenMutateVerdictIntegrityTest` runs the REAL generator in **seconds** against a synthetic `--repo` (every path the script touches is repo-relative) over six scenarios, each guard term watched to FAIL before it was trusted — including the control that proves the refusals discriminate: a legitimate abort naming a fixture still publishes both artifacts at rc 0.

## [0.78.0] - 2026-08-27

### Changed

- **rt#380 (DL-315)** — **the retention payload-nulling leg now ships ON at 7 days**; it defaulted empty, so it was OFF on every install that never tuned it. ⚠ **Operator-visible: existing installs start nulling `webhook_events.payload` older than 7d on the next retention pass.** The row window is **unchanged at 30d** and the rows themselves are untouched — only the payload column of rows older than the payload window. **Measured on two independent installs** before the default moved: 894 MB of a 1.2 GB store on one, **369 MB across 18,931 rows with zero already nulled** on the other, both under a retention that was working correctly the whole time. Neither could discover it without running `SUM(LENGTH(payload))`, which nobody does unprompted — which is the DL-199 argument for `enabled` defaulting TRUE, one level up: *a default nobody sets is exactly why DL-012 never ran*. On the second install the prune took payloads **369 MB → 149 MB** and, after `OPTIMIZE TABLE`, allocation **513 MB → 218 MB**, with row integrity held (18,931 − 813 legitimately aged-out = 18,118) and `bridge:check` rc 0 either side. ⛔ **This is the PAYLOAD window, not the ROW window, and they must not be conflated** — shortening `older_than` to match saves ~16 MB and loses 14% of distinct event types, including gaps `bridge:check` currently REPORTS, which would then read as *fixed* rather than lost. The config comment is re-aimed off *"an optional space optimization for a large store"*, wording that read as a knob for somebody who already had a problem. **The right value is detection latency + response time**, not a guess at replay depth: 7d covers a Friday-evening miss found by a Monday reconcile, and **3d is exactly the window that fails that case**. ⚠ **A SECOND upgrade shape, easy to miss because its preflight currently reads BROKEN:** an install running `BRIDGE_RETENTION_OLDER_THAN=` (empty ⇒ row leg off) **with retention enabled** had no usable window at all — `RetentionConfig::problemWith()` reports MISCONFIGURED only when *both* windows are empty — so `bridge:check` printed `retention: enabled but MISCONFIGURED`, it pruned **nothing**, and an operator could be relying on that as inert. The moved payload default makes the **same `.env`** usable: `problem` becomes null, the preflight flips to `retention: on (null payloads >7d, …)`, and the payload leg starts on the first inbound webhook. **This change therefore also flips a preflight from MISCONFIGURED to `on` and reactivates a fail-safe that had been dormant.** If an empty row window was how you kept retention inert, set `BRIDGE_RETENTION_ENABLED=false` — the supported spelling, and the one `bridge:check` warns about — **before** upgrading. ⛔ **To opt out or widen the window, set `BRIDGE_RETENTION_NULL_PAYLOADS_OLDER_THAN` BEFORE the upgrade and re-run `php artisan config:cache`** — both halves are load-bearing and omitting either loses payloads. **There is no grace period:** `RetentionGate`'s interval marker is a *cache* key, so the first inbound webhook after deploy runs a pass, and `backlogRemains()` clears the marker on a full batch so it keeps draining — the whole >7d payload history can be gone within hours of an upgrade. And a `.env` edit is **inert** under a cached config, so an operator who edits `.env` and reloads FPM has changed nothing. ⚑ `Tests\Support\CheckGolden\GoldenInstall` pinned the old empty value, so the whole golden corpus would have kept asserting a configuration no install has — it now pins the shipped default and 36 fixtures carry the two-leg posture line, with two new fixtures covering the one-leg postures the corpus otherwise lost (every assertion matched the prefix `retention: on (delete >30d`, true of both): `retention-payload-leg-off` for the opt-out install, and `retention-row-leg-off` for the empty-row-window shape above — the arm this release *creates*, and the one whose preflight line moves from `MISCONFIGURED` to `on`. Reported by aimla-pm (rt#380), who also measured and applied it on their fleet first.

- **rt#380 (DL-315) — `bridge:replay` REFUSES an event whose payload retention has nulled, instead of fabricating one.** ⚠ **A behaviour change in the REFUSING direction, and the reason the default change above is safe to ship.** `ReplayCommand` read `is_array($payload) ? $payload : []`, and nothing downstream throws on `[]`: the canonical `InboxOnlyClassifier` returns a *valid* Intent with a null subject and the summary `new card by <who>: <unnamed>`, `DispatchService` appends that line to the agent's **durable `inbox.jsonl`** and on an event-driven seat fires a **live wake carrying invented content**, the errored dispatch is stamped `delivered` with its `error_message` cleared — erasing the record of the original failure — and the command prints `replayed event N` at **rc 0**. Reproduced verbatim before the fix. Replay now exits **1** naming the cause and the knob, and it does so **before any ledger write, `--force` included**, so a refused run leaves every dispatch row exactly as it found it. The predicate is `=== null`, deliberately the **same fact** `bridge:stats` reads as `whereNull('payload')`, so the two surfaces cannot disagree about which rows replay refuses; a wider `! is_array` also refuses a JSON scalar, which no write path in this app can produce (`DispatchService::dispatch()`'s `array $payload` parameter and retention's `update(['payload' => null])` are the only writers) and which SQL has no portable predicate for — so buying that unreachable arm cost a reachable defect, stats labelling the row it could not count `replayable`. The receiver's own surviving copy of the deleted expression (`WebhookController`) goes with it: `parse()` has already decoded the same bytes through `AbstractWebhookAdapter::decodeJson()`, which 400s any non-array, so the ternary was a read-time fallback for a state already refused — the `WebhookAdapter::parse()` contract now states that requirement for a future adapter instead, `docs/provider-adapters.md` stops reproducing the pre-change contract that contradicted it, and a contract test over `WebhookAdapterFactory::SUPPORTED` pins a scalar body to a 400 that stores nothing, so the declaration has a check and not only a sentence. ⚑ **`docs/provider-adapters.md` also stops handing a third-provider author a fatal:** its helper table and BOTH copy-paste `parse()` examples called an `assertDeliveryIdLength` helper that has not existed since the field-length guard was generalised to `assertFieldLengths(EventDto)` — the same phantom this changelog records being fixed in `CLAUDE_GOTCHAS.md`, whose sibling audit stopped at the `CLAUDE_*.md` docs. The table's citations are rewritten into the qualified `Class::member` form `bin/check-doc-refs.php` actually resolves, so the next rename reds CI instead of shipping. **`bridge:inspect` does not refuse** — it dispatches and reconstructs nothing — but it stops printing `json_encode(null)`'s bare `null`, which read as *"the upstream sent nothing"* rather than *"retention removed it"*, and names the cause instead. **`bridge:stats` splits `errored (replayable)`**, which had become a false claim over part of its own count, into that row plus `errored (NOT replayable — event payload nulled by retention)`; both print always, zero included, and the replayable figure is derived by subtraction so the two sum to the errored total — **floored at zero**, because the two COUNTs are still two reads and a retention pass landing between them would otherwise print a negative count of rows. `docs/config-schema.md` and `CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md` both still said the leg was off and neither carried the *"defaults ON — an upgrade starts pruning without operator action"* warning their own `retention.enabled` row two rows above does; both now carry it plus the replay-refusal fact. ⚑ **So did the CONSUMER-facing surface, which is the one whose readers cannot check the code:** `docs/consumer-guide.md` told consuming repos and agents that retention was *"on by default, `older_than` 30d"* and that replay *"re-runs dispatch"* — the wrong window and a promise that now exits 1. Rewritten to state both windows, that the operative recovery window is the payload one, that a row still visible in `bridge:inspect` is not therefore replayable, and that these are per-install operator settings to ask about rather than assume. The five remaining unqualified *"replay recovers it"* lines (`CLAUDE_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/customization.md` ×2, `docs/multi-agent.md`, `CLAUDE_DEPLOYMENT.md`'s errored-row definition) get **a one-clause bound and a pointer, not a sixth copy of the refusal narrative**.

### Security
- **card#7846 (DL-314)** — **the writeback's `getCard` refusal stops pushing a card id it could not read into the alert channel, and its 403 reason code stops naming the one cause it cannot establish.** ⚠ **Operator-visible in two ways; no migration, no config key, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change, and nothing the bridge accepts or writes moves in the permissive direction.** **The mechanism.** `payload.card_id` reaches `kanban_move_card` / `kanban_block_reason` as a literal integer parsed out of **author-controlled text** — a `card#NNNN` token in a PR title or branch ref (`CardTokenGrammar`) — no kanban read behind it and no board scope; `KanbanClient::getCard()` then reads `GET /tasks/{id}.json`, a **GLOBAL** resolve with no board in the path or query, on a kanban whose card id space is shared by every install on the instance. The belongs-to-mapped-board guard that would refuse a foreign card reads `board_id` **out of the row that read returns**, so on a 403 it is structurally unreachable: the tenant boundary's precondition on this path is a **successful cross-tenant read**. It failed closed — but the foreign id had already been sent in a request on this install's credential **and echoed into this install's alert channel**. Observed live on a shared instance: a push on one install alerted `{"repo":"…","outcome":"started","card_id":7756,"reason":"getcard_403_not_visible_to_this_token"}`, where card 7756 is on a board another install owns. ⚑ **Only the bare `card#` path can do this** — a `DL-NNN` correlates through the board-scoped `GET /boards/{board}/tasks/by-ref.json` and cannot return another install's card. **(1) The channel event no longer carries the id** on the `getCard` 4xx arms of both handlers: `card_id` is `null` and a new **`card_id_withheld: true`** key says the omission is deliberate (a bare null already means *the arm had no id* on the malformed-payload and issue-keyed arms — two different next steps). ⛔ **The id is kept in full in the arm's `Log::warning` context**, because the local operator needs it to diagnose and the log is their own surface; it is the *channel event* that can reach a party the id is not about. Same event `type`, same dedup tuple (the flag is constant per `reason`, so it is not in the signature). **(2) The 403 read-reason is renamed `getcard_403_not_visible_to_this_token` → `getcard_403_foreign_card_id_or_token_scope`.** The old slug named only the TOKEN, and the operator who hit the foreign-id case live went auditing their own token's board scope for a card that was never theirs. ⛔ **No discrimination was invented:** a 403 cannot distinguish *a foreign install's id correlated onto this bridge* from *this token's scope missing a board of its own*, so the slug names **both** — the one thing that could decide it is a **board-scoped read of the id against the mapped board, which is deliberately NOT added here** (DL-314 records it as the deferred option, with its costs). ⚠ **A consumer matching the old slug literally will stop matching**, and because dedup keys on `sha1(repo, outcome, reason)` an install already alerted under the old reason will alert **once** more under the new one. `getcard_404_no_such_card` and `getcard_4xx` are unchanged. **Docs:** `docs/writeback.md`'s remediation line for this reason claimed a **`card#`/`DL-NNN`** token could name another install's card — the `DL-NNN` half is **FALSE** and sent an operator hunting a token structurally incapable of the fault; corrected, with the `by-ref` reason stated. **Bound, recorded not fixed:** the two malformed-payload arms (`repo_or_outcome_invalid`, `repo_or_action_invalid`) still push an unverified id — they send no kanban request, so they get no existence answer and disclose strictly less; DL-314 carries the disposition.


### Changed

- **card#7836 (DL-316)** — **`bridge:check`'s client-half green line stops being ONE claim: a call that arrived through the pinned ssh forced command now reports a stronger, narrower statement, and everything else keeps DL-313's wording to the byte.** ⚠ **Run `php artisan migrate`** — one additive nullable column, `board_tools_client_calls.call_provenance`, **no backfill and no other table, column or index touched**. ⛔ **Nothing about what either front door ACCEPTS changes:** `POST /agent-tools/call` and `bridge:tools-call` take exactly the requests they took before; no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change, no severity change, **no exit code moves in either direction**, no check registered (the set stays **38**, so no existing golden fixture moves), `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. **What it closes.** DL-313 shipped the honest wording — *"THAT IS THE CALL, NOT THE CALLER"* — because a pre-merge review **reproduced** a green line from a process with no keypair, no `known_hosts`, no `.mcp.json` and no channel server; the discrimination behind that sentence was named and not built. It is built now: the ssh door records **how the serving process was started** and the leg reports the stronger claim only where it is earned. ⭐ **Only the SSH door measures, and only it can.** `LoopbackOnly` pins the HTTP door's peer to `127.0.0.1` for `--probe-tools` and the seat **alike, by construction**, so that door states its provenance as a constant — ⛔ deliberately, because the PHP process serving it can inherit an ssh session environment perfectly legitimately (`php artisan serve` started inside an ssh session) and reading it there would mint the stronger verdict out of a variable that says nothing about the caller. ⛔ **The predicate is the CONTROLLING TERMINAL, not an environment variable, and the two predicates that look obvious are both MEASURABLY WRONG.** `SSH_CONNECTION` present ALONE is wrong because sshd exports its session variables into the login shell and every descendant inherits them, so an operator hand-running the command in their own ssh shell carries it verbatim. `SSH_CONNECTION` present AND `SSH_TTY` absent — the first fix — is wrong too, and **tmux is the counter-example**: `update-environment` carries `SSH_CONNECTION` into every new pane and **not** `SSH_TTY`, refreshed on every attach, so a tmux server that never had one hands every pane the exact shape that predicate called proven. Measured end to end on the development host: a piped hand-run in such a pane reported the connection variable set, `SSH_TTY` unset, `stream_isatty(STDIN)` false — and a controlling terminal **present**. A pty-less sshd forced command has **no controlling terminal**; every hand-run from a terminal has one and KEEPS it when stdin is a pipe, which is how this door is hand-run (`echo '{...}' | php artisan bridge:tools-call`), so `stream_isatty(STDIN)` could never have been the test either. ⭐ **The predicate, every term's reason, what a `sshd` stamp rules out and the TWO remainders it does not are owned by `app/Bridge/Tools/CallProvenance.php`'s class docblock** — every other surface points there, because the first cut of this feature carried **eleven** hand-maintained restatements and they were all wrong together. ⚠ **The stronger line does NOT name the seat, and prints both remainders itself:** ANY other pty-less ssh invocation (`bridge:check --probe-tools-ssh` and `provision-board-tools.py --self-cert` drive exactly that, indistinguishably from the seat), and a hand-run from a **terminal-less** context that carries `SSH_CONNECTION` — a cron entry or a systemd **user** unit after `systemctl --user import-environment`, an agent tool harness, or a `setsid` wrapper. ⛔ **The only complete fix is a MARKED request** so the probes suppress their own stamp, which changes what a front door accepts and was **not built** under this card. ⚑ **A pre-upgrade row does NOT read as proven:** the column is nullable with no backfill, because both backfills are lies — `not_sshd` asserts a measurement nobody took and `sshd` promotes exactly the rows this exists to stop over-reading — so an existing ledger keeps the weaker verdict until each seat calls again. ⛔ **Names and booleans only:** `SSH_CONNECTION` is a client IP, a client port and this host's own address and port, and the row is printed verbatim into an operator line — only **presence** crosses the boundary, and the pins are written over the whole row and the whole rendered line rather than over the one column. ⚑ **A fact the serving process cannot ESTABLISH is a third state, not a measurement:** each term is `?bool` and only a measurement earns the stronger claim, so an install whose run-user `php.ini` carries an `open_basedir` — which denies the probe `/dev/tty` **and** `/proc` alike — records `not_sshd` and keeps DL-313's line for every call, including a genuine one. A missed strong claim, never a false one. **Operator-visible:** an agent whose last successful call came through the ssh door reads `client half REPORTED THROUGH THE SSH DOOR — …` instead of `client half REPORTED — …`; every other agent's line is unchanged.

## [0.77.0] - 2026-08-26

### Added

- **card#7756 (DL-313)** — **`bridge:check` gains the first leg that says anything about the CALLING SEAT's half of the board-tools door, so a never-wired seat and a merely-idle one stop rendering as the bridge's problem.** ⚠ **Run `php artisan migrate`** — one new table, `board_tools_client_calls`, one row per agent, no backfill and nothing else touched. Every board-tools leg the command had observes the **bridge** side (the pinned `authorized_keys` line, the DL-225 transport default, the writeback token's board window); none could see the seat's keypair, its seeded `known_hosts`, the `BRIDGE_TOOLS_*` entries in its own `.mcp.json`, or its deployed channel server — so a seat that was **never wired client-side** and a seat whose pinned line is **merely absent** printed the same lines, and the two take **opposite remedies**. ⛔ **The bridge does not go and look:** reading the seat's `.mcp.json` was the proposed mechanism and is refused, because an account may only read its own files — the same rule that keeps `channel.server_path` an operator declaration rather than an inference (DL-229). The seat **reports by calling**: `BoardToolDispatcher` already logged every successful call and now also persists one, and a SEAT reaching that success point has exercised its entire client chain. **No new tool and no seat-side change of any kind.** ⛔ **The row names the agent the door opened FOR and cannot name the caller** — `bridge:check --probe-tools`, `bin/provision-board-tools.py --self-cert` and a hand-run `bridge:tools-call --agent=X` on the bridge host stamp it identically, and the runbook's own verify step is the first of those, one step BEFORE the channel server is restarted — so the green line states that bound in its own text (*"THAT IS THE CALL, NOT THE CALLER … a recorded call means the door OPENED — not necessarily that the seat opened it"*) instead of claiming a wired seat. ⭐ **TWO outcomes, not three** — *"never wired"* is **withdrawn as an observable state**, because a seat that can report is by definition wired, so never-wired and idle are one absence here: a **fresh** call reports `ok` and **names the age on the green line** (*"a successful board-tools call for this agent was recorded 3h ago, over ssh"* — an operator judging the number beats a boolean they must trust), while **no record or a stale one** reports `unvalidated` and **says in its own text that this is NOT evidence the seat is unwired**, that the bridge cannot tell idle from broken, and that the remedy is to **ask the seat for one call** rather than re-provision it. **Never `fail`, never `warn`** — a `fail` would exit non-zero over a seat that is quiet by choice and a `warn` would assert a fault the bridge cannot establish — and **no new `Severity` case**, so the exhaustive-`match` property and the exit contract are untouched by construction. New knob `BRIDGE_BOARD_TOOLS_CLIENT_HALF_TTL` (default **7 days**): a display threshold only, and a non-positive value falls back to the default rather than staling every stamp the instant it is written. ⛔ **The store is a DB table and not a state-dir file, and the reason is measured rather than assumed:** the two front doors run as **different OS users** — `bridge:tools-call --agent=` is spawned by sshd as `board_tools.ssh_account`, `POST /agent-tools/call` is served by the PHP-FPM pool user — so a shared file under the 0700 config dir would leave the two transports with two answers or one with none, while both users already hold the same DB credential. The row holds **the agent name, the transport, and two timestamps and nothing else** — no secret, no token, no config value — because it is printed verbatim into an operator line. **Recording can never break the call:** it runs after the board work, so every throw is caught and logged (the `BoardDivergenceLedger` posture, DL-300), asserted against a genuinely unmigrated connection returning the driver's own `no such table`. ⚠ **Operator-visible:** the registered check set goes **37 → 38**, so every run's inventory line moves (all 37 golden fixtures regenerated, plus one new `board-tools-client-half-wired` fixture that is the `board-tools-http-enabled` install differing in exactly the one new line); an install with an enabled `board_tools` block and no recorded call gains one plain `unvalidated` line per such agent and one to its closing tally. **No exit code moves in either direction**, no token-scope change, no receiver accept/reject change, `--format=json` `schema` stays **1**. **Test-integrity consequence, the DL-300 shape a second time:** a success-path primitive writing a durable row means `board_tools_client_calls` joins `Tests\TestCase::COMMITTABLE_TABLES` and the three `tests/Feature/AgentTools/` classes that dispatch a successful call gained `RefreshDatabase`, or those rows would COMMIT on the MariaDB legs and outlive the test while SQLite `:memory:` stayed green.

### Changed

- **#593** — the managed `coord:solo-orientation` block in `CLAUDE_AGENTBOARD.md` re-synced from coord plugin v0.43.0 to **v0.44.0**. Docs only; the block gains the `coord:install-rules` span (the one region both auto-loaded and preserved across syncs), a session-end handoff step that ends at a verdict rather than at the write, and the note that a dispatched subagent does not hold a `/clear` provided the handoff enumerates it.
- **#582 · #581 · #583 · #584** — dependency bumps: `laravel/framework` 13.25.0 → 13.26.1, `mockery/mockery` (dev) 1.6.12 → 1.6.15, and the `PupFuzz/agent-board-toolkit` `release-artifacts` + `promote` composite actions 0.28.0 → 0.29.0. Lockfile/workflow-pin only — no `app/` change, no migration, no config key.

## [0.76.0] - 2026-08-25

### Changed

- **Framework orientation block re-synced to coord v0.43.0** (`/coord:update` area 4). Docs-only: the managed `coord:solo-orientation` block gains the note that only `## Who you are` and `## Read at session start` are auto-surfaced to a session, so a standing rule written elsewhere in the doc silently never loads. No executable line changes; the project addendum below the block is untouched.
- **Framework orientation block re-synced to coord v0.44.0** (`/coord:update` area 4). Docs-only, wholly inside the managed `coord:solo-orientation` markers; no executable line changes. The block gains **`coord:install-rules`** — a marker pair inside `## Read at session start` that is the one span of the doc both AUTO-LOADED into a session and PRESERVED across `orientation-sync`, which is what makes an install standing rule survivable (elsewhere in the managed block a rule is erased by the next sync; below the `PROJECT ADDENDUM` divider it survives but never loads). It also gains two session-end/context rules: the handoff step ends at a **verdict, not at the write** — `handoff-check.py --auto` reds when a handoff claim contradicts live `gh`/`kbcard`/`git` state, and `handoff-write-check.py` now fires on whichever tool call changed the file — and a **dispatched subagent in flight does not hold a `/clear`**, provided the handoff enumerates the running subagents, since subagents survive the clear and their completions land in the next session.

### Fixed

- **card#7597** — **the PR-title correlation-key lint subscribes to `synchronize`, so a PR that got
  no first run can recover.** GitHub creates **no workflow runs at all** for a PR whose merge ref it
  cannot compute (the base moved and the PR is conflicting), and the conflict is then cleared by a
  push — a `synchronize`, the one event `pr-title-lint.yml` omitted by design. A PR opened while
